Then the following Berry-Esseen bound holds

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\bm{G}_{\text{F}}(t)-\Phi(t)\right|\leq C{n_{z}}^{-1/2}.

Proof.

Following similar steps used to prove Theorem
3
, we first decompose the numerator

\displaystyle\bm{y}_{z}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{y}}=\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{Z}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{y}}+\epsilon_{z}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{y}}.

Note that the first term is deterministic in our setting, we therefore only need to derive the CLT for the second quantity. By Lemma S
6
, we immediately have

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\epsilon_{z}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{y}}}{\sigma_{\epsilon_{z}}\|\widehat{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}<t\right)-\Phi(t)\right|\leq c\sqrt{\frac{\tau\mathbb{E}(\epsilon_{z_{i}}^{4})\sum_{i=1}^{n_{z}}\widehat{\bm{y}}_{i}^{4}}{\sigma_{\epsilon_{z}}^{4}\|\widehat{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{4}}}=O_{p}(n_{z}^{-1/2}).

It follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{y}_{z}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{y}}-\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{Z}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{y}}}{\sigma_{\epsilon_{z}}\|\widehat{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}<t\right)-\Phi(t)\right|\leq O_{p}(n_{z}^{-1/2}).

By Chebyshev’s inequality, for
\delta\in(0,1/2)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\|\bm{y}_{z}\|_{2}^{2}-(\|\bm{Z}\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}+n_{z}\sigma_{\epsilon_{z}}^{2})\right|<n_{z}^{1-\delta}\right)\geq 1-O_{p}(n_{z}^{-1+2\delta}).

This concludes the limit for the denominator. With some rearrangements of the terms, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{n_{z}}\frac{\sqrt{\|\bm{Z}\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}/n_{z}+\sigma_{\epsilon_{z}}^{2}}}{\sigma_{\epsilon_{z}}}\frac{\bm{y}_{z}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{y}}-\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{Z}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{y}}}{\|\widehat{\bm{y}}\|_{2}\|\bm{y}_{z}\|_{2}}<t\right)-\Phi(t)\right|\leq O_{p}(n_{z}^{-1/2}).

Replacing
{\sigma_{\epsilon_{z}}^{2}}
with
\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}(1/h_{\bm{\beta}_{z}}^{2}-1)
, we obtain the desired naive CLT.
∎

Note that we have
\eta_{\text{F}}\geq 1
. Therefore, an immediate result following Lemma S
1
is that if we treat
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}
as fixed, we will underestimate the true variance of our estimators, leading to overconfidence in the accuracy of the confidence intervals and hypothesis testing. This conclusion holds for all of
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{M}}
,
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{W}}(\lambda)
, and
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{R}}(\lambda)
.
The underestimation of variance arises from omitting the fluctuation within the estimator, specifically the fluctuations of
\bm{\beta},\bm{X},\bm{\epsilon}
for
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{M}}
, fluctuations of
\bm{\beta},\bm{X},\bm{\epsilon},\bm{W}
for
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{W}}(\lambda)
, and fluctuations of
\bm{\beta},\bm{X},\bm{\epsilon}
for
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{R}}(\lambda)
.

Appendix S.2
Results of traditional ridge estimator

We start by introducing the additional Gaussian assumptions and notations.

Assumption S1
.

Adopt Assumptions
1
-
2
, we further assume
\bm{X}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times p}
with i.i.d entries follow from
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
independent from
\bm{\epsilon}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{n})
. Similar conditions hold for
\bm{Z}_{0}
and
\sigma_{\epsilon_{z}}^{2}
.
Additionally, we assume
n\asymp n_{z}\asymp p\asymp m
, and
p/n\rightarrow\phi_{n}\in\mathbb{R}
as
p\rightarrow\infty
.

For
\forall\lambda>0
, we introduce the following definitions of the Stieltjes transform for the training data, including

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{n}\coloneqq m_{n}(-\lambda)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathfrak{r}_{n}\coloneqq\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{2}/\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime},

where
\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}=d(m_{n}(-\lambda))/d\lambda
.
For the special case
\bm{\Sigma}=\mathbb{I}_{p}
, we further have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{n}(-\lambda,\bm{\Sigma}=\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})=\frac{\sqrt{(\lambda+\phi_{n}-1)^{2}+4\lambda}-(\lambda+\phi_{n}-1)}{2\lambda}

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{r}_{n}(-\lambda,\bm{\Sigma}=\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})=1-4\phi_{n}\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{(\lambda+\phi_{n}-1)^{2}+4\lambda}+(\lambda+\phi_{n}+1)}\right\}^{2}.

Moreover, if we consider the optimal ridge parameter
\lambda^{*}=\phi_{n}(1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})/h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}
, we can rewrite
\mathfrak{m}_{n}
and
\mathfrak{r}_{n}
into some parameter depends only on
h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}
and
\phi_{n}
:

\mathfrak{m}_{n}(-\lambda,\bm{\Sigma}=\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})=\frac{2h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{\sqrt{(\phi_{n}+h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})^{2}-4h_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}\phi_{n}}+\phi_{n}-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}

and

\mathfrak{r}_{n}(-\lambda,\bm{\Sigma}=\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})=1-4\phi_{n}\left\{\frac{h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{\sqrt{(\phi_{n}+h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})^{2}-4h_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}\phi_{n}}+\phi_{n}+h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}\right\}^{2}.

S.2.1
Individual-level uncertainty

Corollary S
1
below presents the CLT results for
\bm{z}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{R}}(\lambda)
in the special case
\bm{\Sigma}=\mathbb{I}_{p}
.

Corollary S1
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
2
, S
1
and
\bm{\Sigma}=\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}
, as
\min(n,p,m)\to\infty
, conditioning on the testing data point
\bm{z}
and genetic effect
\bm{\beta}
, let

\displaystyle\bm{F}_{\text{R}_{0}}(t)=\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma_{\text{R}_{0}}^{-1}\sqrt{n}\left(\bm{z}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{R}}(\lambda)-\mu_{\text{R}_{0}}\right)<t\right),

where

\displaystyle\mu_{\text{R}_{0}}=\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{n}}{1+\mathfrak{m}_{n}}\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\beta}

and

\displaystyle\sigma_{\text{R}_{0}}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{\phi_{n}}{\lambda\mathfrak{r}_{n}(1+\mathfrak{m}_{n})^{2}}\left\{\left(\frac{\lambda}{\phi_{n}}-\frac{1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}\right)\|\bm{z}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{(1+\mathfrak{m}_{n})^{2}}(\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\beta})^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\lambda\mathfrak{m}_{n}}\left\{\frac{1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}\|\bm{z}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\phi_{n}}{\lambda\mathfrak{m}_{n}(1+\mathfrak{m}_{n})^{3}}(\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\beta})^{2}\right\}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\bm{F}_{\text{R}}(t)-\Phi(t)\right|\to 0.

Moreover, under the optimal ridge parameter
\lambda^{*}=\phi_{n}(1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})/h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}

[
62
]
, the mean and variance become

\displaystyle\mu_{\text{R}_{0}}=\frac{2h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{\sqrt{(\phi_{n}+h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})^{2}-4h_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}\phi_{n}}+\phi_{n}+h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\sigma_{\text{R}_{0}}^{2}=\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}\phi_{n}}\|\bm{z}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{(1+\mathfrak{m}_{n})^{3}(1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})}\left\{\frac{1}{\phi_{n}\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{2}}\frac{h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{r}_{n}(1+\mathfrak{m}_{n})}\right\}(\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\beta})^{2}.

Corollary S
1
quantifies the mean and variance of
\bm{z}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{R}}(\lambda)
. The influence of the resolvent
(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}
is explicitly characterized by
\mathfrak{m}_{n}
and
\mathfrak{r}_{n}
, both of which have closed-form expressions when
\bm{\Sigma}=\mathbb{I}_{p}
. The bias of
\bm{z}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{R}}(\lambda)
is linearly quantified as
(1+\mathfrak{m}_{n})^{-1}\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\beta}
, which suggests that genetically predicted values are shrunk towards zero by the degrees controlled by
\mathfrak{m}_{n}
.
It is worth mentioning that our analysis does not exclude the case when
\lambda\to 0
. Therefore, our results may also be applied to ridge-less estimators
[
29
]
to understand model overfitting behaviors.
In addition, because we use techniques for de-biased estimators with an additional Gaussian assumption, we will not have an explicit Berry-Esseen type upper bound.

Briefly, in the de-biased estimator framework
[
8
]
, the Wasserstein distance between the de-biased estimator and a standard Gaussian random variable is upper bounded by a quantity determined by the gradient of the function related to the training data. This quantity is of order
o_{p}(1)
, and therefore, no explicit convergence rate is provided.
Next, we provide the results for general
\bm{\Sigma}
.

Definition S.1
.

For any
1\leq i\leq 2
, we introduce two sequence parameters

\displaystyle\ell_{i}\coloneqq\frac{{\rm Tr}\left\{(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-i}\right\}}{p}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\eth_{i}\coloneqq\frac{{\rm Tr}\left\{(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-i}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\right\}}{p},

and two quantities derived from
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}=1-(1-\ell_{1})\phi_{n}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathfrak{h}=\phi_{n}\left\{\ell_{1}-\frac{\lambda\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}}\left(\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}\right)\right\}.

Theorem S1
.

Under Assumption
1
-
2
, S
1
, as
\min(n,p,m)\to\infty
, conditioning on the testing data point
\bm{z}
and genetic effect
\bm{\beta}
, let

\displaystyle\bm{F}_{\text{R}}(t)=\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma_{\text{R}}^{-1}\sqrt{n}\left\{\bm{z}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{R}}(\lambda)-\mu_{\text{R}}\right\}<t\right),

where

\displaystyle\mu_{\text{R}}=\bm{z}^{\top}\left\{\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}-\frac{\lambda}{\mathfrak{g}}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}\left(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}-(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\right)\right\}\bm{\beta}

and

\displaystyle\sigma_{\text{R}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{h}\mathfrak{s}_{1}^{2}/\mathfrak{g}^{2}+\left[\mathfrak{g}\left(\frac{1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}}\right)+\frac{\lambda\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}}\left\{\lambda\mathfrak{m}_{n}\mathfrak{s}_{2}-\left(\frac{1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}\right)\phi_{n}\left(\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}\right){\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\cdot\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\bm{z}\|_{2}^{2}/\mathfrak{g}^{2}.

Here

\displaystyle\mathfrak{s}_{1}=\bm{z}^{\top}(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\beta}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathfrak{s}_{2}=\bm{\beta}^{\top}(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\bm{F}_{\text{R}}(t)-\Phi(t)\right|\to 0.

Moreover, under the optimal ridge parameter
\lambda^{*}=\phi_{n}(1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})/h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}
, the mean and variance become

\displaystyle\mu_{\text{R}}

\displaystyle=\bm{z}^{\top}\left\{\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}-\frac{\phi_{n}(1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})}{h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\mathfrak{g}}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}\left(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}-(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\right)\right\}\bm{\beta}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\sigma_{\text{R}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{h}\mathfrak{s}_{1}^{2}/\mathfrak{g}^{2}+\frac{\phi_{n}(1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})}{h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}\left[\frac{\mathfrak{g}}{\phi_{n}}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}}\frac{\phi_{n}(1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})}{h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}\left\{\mathfrak{m}_{n}\mathfrak{s}_{2}-\left(\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}\right)\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\cdot\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\bm{z}\|_{2}^{2}/\mathfrak{g}^{2}.

S.2.2
Cohort-level uncertainty

In the special case
\bm{\Sigma}=\mathbb{I}_{p}
, we have the following results for prediction accuracy of ridge estimator.

Corollary S2
.

Under Assumption
1
-
2
, S
1
and
\bm{\Sigma}=\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}
, as
\min(n,n_{z},p,m)\to\infty
, let

\displaystyle\bm{G}_{\text{R}_{0}}(t)=\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{\eta_{\text{R}_{0}}n_{z}}\left(A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{R}_{0}}(\lambda))-\widetilde{A}_{\text{R}_{0}}\right)<t\right),

with

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{\text{R}_{0}}=\mathfrak{m}_{n}h_{\bm{\beta}_{z}}\left\{\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{2}+\frac{1-\mathfrak{r}_{n}}{\mathfrak{r}_{n}}+\left(\mathfrak{m}_{n}+\frac{\mathfrak{r}_{n}-1}{\mathfrak{r}_{n}}\right)\frac{\phi_{n}(1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})}{\lambda h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}\right\}^{-1/2}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\eta_{\text{R}_{0}}=\frac{\varsigma_{1}}{\varsigma_{1}+\varsigma_{2}+\varsigma_{3}+\varsigma_{4}},

where

\displaystyle\varsigma_{1}=\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}h_{\bm{\beta}_{z}}^{2}}\left\{\left(\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{2}+\frac{1-\mathfrak{r}_{n}}{\mathfrak{r}_{n}}\right)+\left(\mathfrak{m}_{n}+\frac{\mathfrak{r}_{n}-1}{\mathfrak{r}_{n}}\right)\frac{\phi_{n}(1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})}{h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}\lambda^{-1}\right\},\quad\varsigma_{2}=2\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{2},

\displaystyle\varsigma_{3}=\frac{n_{z}}{n}\left[\frac{\phi_{n}}{\lambda^{2}(1+\mathfrak{m}_{n})}\left\{\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{2}}-\frac{\lambda}{(1+\mathfrak{m}_{n})\mathfrak{r}_{n}}\right\}+\frac{(1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})(1+\mathfrak{m}_{n})^{2}}{\lambda\mathfrak{m}_{n}}+\frac{h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{\mathfrak{r}_{n}}-\frac{\phi_{n}(1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})}{\lambda\mathfrak{r}_{n}}\right],\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\varsigma_{4}=\frac{n_{z}\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{2}}{m}\left\{\frac{\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\beta}^{4}\right)p^{2}}{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}}-1\right\}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\bm{G}_{\text{R}_{0}}(t)-\Phi(t)\right|\to 0.

Moreover, under the optimal ridge parameter
\lambda^{*}=\phi_{n}(1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})/h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}

[
62
]
, we can update the results as follows

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{\text{R}_{0}}=\sqrt{2}h_{\bm{\beta}_{z}}h_{\bm{\beta}}\left\{\left\{(\phi_{n}+h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})^{2}-4h_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}\phi_{n}\right\}^{1/2}+\phi_{n}+h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\right\}^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\varsigma_{1}=\frac{h_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}}{(1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})^{2}h_{\bm{\beta}_{z}}^{2}\phi_{n}^{2}\mathfrak{m}_{n}(1+\mathfrak{m}_{n})},\quad\varsigma_{2}=2\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{2},

\displaystyle\varsigma_{3}=\frac{n_{z}}{n}\left[\frac{h_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}}{(1+\mathfrak{m}_{n})\phi_{n}(1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})^{2}}\left\{\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{2}}-\frac{\phi_{n}(1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})}{(1+\mathfrak{m}_{n})\mathfrak{r}_{n}h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}\right\}+\frac{h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}(1+\mathfrak{m}_{n})^{2}}{\phi_{n}\mathfrak{m}_{n}}\right],\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\varsigma_{4}=\frac{n_{z}\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{2}}{m}\left\{\frac{\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\beta}^{4}\right)p^{2}}{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}}-1\right\}.

Next, we state our CLT results for
A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{R}}(\lambda))
with general
\bm{\Sigma}
.

Theorem S2
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
2
, S
1
, as
\min(n,n_{z},p,m)\to\infty
, let

\displaystyle\bm{G}_{\text{R}}(t)=\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{\eta_{\text{R}}n_{z}}\left(A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{R}}(\lambda))-\widetilde{A}_{\text{R}}\right)<t\right),

with

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{\text{R}}=\tau_{0}\tau_{1}^{-1/2}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\eta_{\text{R}}=\frac{\tau_{1}}{\tau_{1}+2\tau_{2}^{2}+\tau_{3}+\tau_{4}^{2}},

where

\displaystyle\tau_{0}=\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\left\{\gamma_{1}-\frac{\lambda}{\mathfrak{g}}\left(\frac{m}{p}-\eth_{1}\right)\right\},\quad\tau_{1}=\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2},\quad\tau_{2}=\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\left\{\gamma_{1}-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}}\left(\frac{m}{p}-\eth_{1}\right)\right\},

\displaystyle\tau_{3}=\frac{n_{z}\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}}{n\mathfrak{g}^{2}}\left[\mathfrak{h}\left\{\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}}\left(\frac{m}{p}-\eth_{1}\right)\right\}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\left\{\frac{1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}\gamma_{1}\mathfrak{g}+\frac{\lambda\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}}\left\{\lambda\left(\eth_{1}-\eth_{2}\right)-\frac{1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}\gamma_{1}\phi_{n}(\ell_{1}-\ell_{2})\right\}\right\}\right],

\displaystyle\mbox{and}\quad\tau_{4}^{2}=\frac{n_{z}\lambda^{2}}{\mathfrak{g}^{2}}\left\{\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\beta}^{4}\right)-\frac{3\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}}{p^{2}}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathfrak{N}_{i,i}^{2}+\frac{2\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}}{p^{2}}{\rm Tr}(\mathfrak{N}^{2})\right\}.

Here

\displaystyle\zeta_{1}=\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\left\{\gamma_{1}-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}}\left\{2\left(\frac{m}{p}-\eth_{1}\right)-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{r}_{n}}\left(\eth_{1}-\eth_{2}\right)\right\}\right\}+\frac{1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\gamma_{1}\phi_{n}}{\lambda\mathfrak{m}_{n}}\left\{\left(1-\ell_{1}\right)-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{r}_{n}}\left(\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}\right)\right\},

\displaystyle\zeta_{2}=\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{h_{\bm{\beta}_{z}}^{2}}\gamma_{1},\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathfrak{N}=\left\{\frac{\mathfrak{g}}{\lambda}\bm{\Sigma}-\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\right\}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}.

Then the following quantitative CLT holds

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\bm{G}_{\text{R}}(t)-\Phi(t)\right|\to 0.

Note that
\eth_{1}
and
\eth_{2}
are defined in Definition
S.1
.
Moreover, under the optimal ridge parameter
\lambda^{*}=\phi_{n}(1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})/h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}

[
62
]
, we have the following updates

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{\text{R}}=h_{\bm{\beta}_{z}}\gamma_{1}^{-1/2}\left\{\gamma_{1}-\frac{\phi_{n}(1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})}{h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\mathfrak{g}}\left(\frac{m}{p}-\eth_{1}\right)\right\}\left[\gamma_{1}-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}}\left\{2\left(\frac{m}{p}-\eth_{1}\right)+\frac{1}{\mathfrak{r}_{n}}\left(\eth_{1}-\eth_{2}\right)\right\}+\right.

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}}\left\{\left(1-\ell_{1}\right)-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{r}_{n}}\left(\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}\right)\right\}\right]^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\tau_{3}=\frac{n_{z}\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}}{n\mathfrak{g}^{2}}\left[\mathfrak{h}\left\{\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}}\left(\frac{m}{p}-\eth_{1}\right)\right\}^{2}+\frac{\phi_{n}\gamma_{1}(1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})}{h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}\left\{\frac{\gamma_{1}\mathfrak{g}}{\phi_{n}}+\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}}\frac{\phi_{n}(1-h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})}{h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}\left\{\left(\eth_{1}-\eth_{2}\right)-\gamma_{1}(\ell_{1}-\ell_{2})\right\}\right\}\right],

\displaystyle\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\zeta_{1}=\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\left[\gamma_{1}-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}}\left\{2\left(\frac{m}{p}-\eth_{1}\right)-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{r}_{n}}\left(\eth_{1}-\eth_{2}\right)\right\}+\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}}\left\{\left(1-\ell_{1}\right)-\frac{1}{\mathfrak{r}_{n}}\left(\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}\right)\right\}\right].

Here
\tau_{1}
emerges naturally from the Gaussian distribution originated from the randomness of
\epsilon_{z}
and
\tau_{2}
follows from the Gaussian distribution generated by the randomness of
\bm{Z}_{0}
. Moreover,
\tau_{3}
captures the Gaussian generated by the randomness of
\bm{X}_{0}
and
\epsilon
, which is obtained using the asymptotic normal approximation of de-biased estimators
[
8
]
. In addition,
\tau_{4}
follows from the Gaussian distribution generated by the randomness of
\bm{\beta}
, which is obtained using Theorem
2
. Similar to Theorems
3
and
5
, we have
0<\eta_{\text{R}}<1
.

Appendix S.3
Key concentration lemmas

In this section, we present several key concentration lemmas useful in proving our quantitative CLTs.

Lemma S1

(CLT for quadratic functional)
.

Under Assumption
1
, assume
\bm{X}_{0}
is a
n\times p
matrix and some deterministic vector
\bm{a}
and
\bm{b}
, then

\frac{\left\langle\bm{a},\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{b}\right\rangle-n\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{b}}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}\overset{d}{\to}\mathcal{N}(0,1),

where

\sigma^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left(x_{0}^{4}-3\right)\sum_{i=1}^{p}\bm{a}_{i}^{2}\bm{b}_{i}^{2}+2\left(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{b}\right)^{2}+\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{b}\|_{2}^{2}.

Similar results also hold for
\bm{Z}_{0}
with scale
n_{z}\times p
.

Proof.

Notice that

\left\langle\bm{a},\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{b}\right\rangle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0_{i}}\bm{b}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0_{i}},

where
\bm{x}_{0_{i}}
is
i_{th}
i.i.d. row vectors of
\bm{X}_{0}
.
We have
\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0}\bm{b}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0}\right)=\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{b}
and
\mathbb{E}\{(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0})^{2}(\bm{b}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0})^{2}\}=\mathbb{E}(x_{0}^{4}-3)\sum_{i=1}^{p}\bm{a}_{i}^{2}\bm{b}_{i}^{2}+2(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{b})^{2}+\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{b}\|_{2}^{2}
.

Denote

\bm{X}_{n,i}=\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0_{i}}\bm{b}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0_{i}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{b}}{\bm{\sigma}\sqrt{n}},

we will check Lyapunov’s conditions for the CLT result

1.

\mathbb{E}(\bm{X}_{n,i})=0
;

2.

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}(\bm{X}_{n,i}^{2})\to 1
when
n\to\infty
; and

3.

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}(\bm{X}_{n,i}^{4})\to 0
when
n\to\infty
.

The first two conditions follow trivially from our definition of
\bm{X}_{n,i}
. Now we examine the fourth moment of
\bm{X}_{n,i}
. Notice that

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}(\bm{X}_{n,i}^{4})=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left\{(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0}\bm{b}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{b})^{4}\right\}}{\sigma^{4}n}\leq\frac{M\left\{\mathbb{E}\left\{(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0})^{4}(\bm{b}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0})^{4}\right\}+(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{b})^{4}\right\}}{\sigma^{4}n}

for some large constant
M
. Moreover, we have

\mathbb{E}\left\{(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0})^{4}(\bm{b}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0})^{4}\right\}\leq\lambda\mathbb{E}\left\{(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{x})^{8}\right\}+\frac{1}{\lambda}\mathbb{E}\left\{(\bm{b}^{\top}\bm{x})^{8}\right\}

for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
.
With Assumption
1
, the order of

\mathbb{E}\{(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{x})^{8}\}
and

\mathbb{E}\{(\bm{b}^{\top}\bm{x})^{8}\}
only depends on terms with the most summation,
which are
\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{8}
and
\|\bm{b}\|_{2}^{8}
, respectively.
Therefore, we can further bound the right-hand side with the dominant terms

\mathbb{E}\left\{(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0})^{4}(\bm{b}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0})^{4}\right\}\leq\lambda N\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{8}+\frac{1}{\lambda}N\|\bm{b}\|_{2}^{8}

for some large constant
N
.
Since
\sigma^{2}\geq O_{p}(\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{b}\|_{2}^{2})\geq r\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{b}\|_{2}^{2}
for some small
r
, we have

\frac{M\mathbb{E}\left\{(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0})^{4}(\bm{b}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0})^{4}\right\}}{\sigma^{4}n}\leq\frac{M}{n}\frac{\lambda\left(N\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{8}\right)+\left(N\|\bm{b}\|_{2}^{8}\right)/\lambda}{r^{2}(\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{4}\|\bm{b}\|_{2}^{4})}=\frac{1}{n}\frac{MN}{r^{2}}\left(\lambda\frac{\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{4}}{\|\bm{b}\|_{2}^{4}}+\frac{1}{\lambda}\frac{\|\bm{b}\|_{2}^{4}}{\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{4}}\right).

By taking
\lambda=\|\bm{b}\|_{2}^{4}/\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{4}
, we have

\frac{M\mathbb{E}\left\{(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0})^{4}(\bm{b}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0})^{4}\right\}}{\sigma^{4}n}\leq\frac{1}{n}\frac{MN}{r^{2}}\to 0,

as
n\to\infty
.
Since
\sigma^{2}\geq O_{p}\{(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{b})^{2}\}\geq s(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{b})^{2}
for some small
s
, we have

\frac{(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{b})^{4}}{\sigma^{4}n}\leq\frac{(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{b})^{4}}{s^{2}(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{b})^{4}n}=\frac{1}{s^{2}n}\to 0

as
n\to\infty
.
Combining two results above, we conclude the third Lyapunov’s condition.
∎

Lemma S2

(Quantitative CLT for quadratic functional)
.

Under Assumption
1
, assume
\bm{X}_{0}
is a
n\times p
matrix and some deterministic vector
\bm{a}
and
\bm{b}
.
Let

\bm{F}_{n}(t)=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left\langle\bm{a},\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{b}\right\rangle-n\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{b}}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}<t\right),

we have

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}|\bm{F}_{n}(t)-\Phi(t)|\leq c\sqrt{\frac{\tau}{n}},

where

\sigma^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{x}_{0}^{4}-3\right)\sum_{i=1}^{p}\bm{a}_{i}^{2}\bm{b}_{i}^{2}+2(\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{b})^{2}+\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{b}\|_{2}^{2}

for
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
and
c
is some constant.
Similar results also hold for
\bm{Z}_{0}
with scale
n_{z}\times p
.

Proof.

As in Lemma S
1
, denote

\bm{X}_{n,i}=\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0_{i}}\bm{b}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0_{i}}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{b}}{\sigma\sqrt{n}}.

We have already shown that

1.

\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{X}_{n,i}\right)=0
;

2.

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{X}_{n,i}^{2}\right)\to 1
when
n\to\infty
; and

3.

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{X}_{n,i}^{4}\right)\leq O_{p}(n^{-1})
.

Define Lyapunov coefficients as

L_{t}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left|\bm{X}_{n,i}\right|^{t},

and by using the Berry-Esseen Theorem with Lyapunov coefficient
L_{4}
, we have the following result

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}|\bm{F}_{n}(t)-\Phi(t)|\leq cL_{3}\leq c\sqrt{L_{4}}

\displaystyle\implies\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}|\bm{F}_{n}(t)-\Phi(t)|\leq c\sqrt{\frac{\tau}{n}}

for some constant
\tau
decided by the exact value of
L_{4}
.
∎

Lemma S3

(CLT for linear functional)
.

Under Assumption
1
, assume
\bm{X}_{0}
is a
n\times p
matrix and some non-deterministic vector
\bm{a}
independent with
\bm{X}_{0}
and deterministic vector
\bm{b}
where
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}(\bm{a}_{i}^{4})/\mathbb{E}(\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2})^{2}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, we have

\frac{\left\langle\bm{a},\bm{X}_{0}\bm{b}\right\rangle}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left(\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2}\right)}\|\bm{b}\|_{2}}\overset{d}{\to}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

Similar results also hold for
\bm{Z}_{0}
with scale
n_{z}\times p
.

Proof.

Similarly, it is possible to decompose the desired quantity into i.i.d copies

\left\langle\bm{a},\bm{X}_{0}\bm{b}\right\rangle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{a}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{b}.

We can show that
\mathbb{E}(\bm{a}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{b})=0
and

\mathbb{E}(\bm{a}_{i}^{2}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{b}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{b})=\mathbb{E}(\bm{a}_{i}^{2})\|\bm{b}\|_{2}^{2}
.
Denote

\bm{X}_{n,i}=\frac{\bm{a}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{b}}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left(\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2}\right)}\|\bm{b}\|_{2}},

we proceed by checking Lyapunov’s conditions

1.

\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{X}_{n,i}\right)=0;

2.

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{X}_{n,i}^{2}\right)=\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{a}_{i}^{2}\right)\|\bm{b}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\ /\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\|\bm{b}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}=1;
and

3.

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{X}_{n,i}^{4}\right)=\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{a}_{i}^{4}\right)\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{x}_{0}^{4}-3\right)\sum_{j=1}^{p}\bm{b}_{j}^{4}+3\|\bm{b}\|_{2}^{4}\right\}\right]/\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}\|\bm{b}\|_{2}^{4}\right\}\\
\qquad=O_{p}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{a}_{i}^{4}\right)\|\bm{b}\|_{2}^{4}\right)/\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}\|\bm{b}\|_{2}^{4}\right\}\to 0
as
n\to\infty.

∎

Lemma S4

(Quantitative CLT for linear functional)
.

Under Assumption
1
, assume
\bm{X}_{0}
is a
n\times p
matrix and some non-deterministic vector
\bm{a}
independent with
\bm{X}_{0}
and deterministic vector
\bm{b}
.
Let

\bm{F}_{n}(t)=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left\langle\bm{a},\bm{X}_{0}\bm{b}\right\rangle}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left(\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2}\right)}\|\bm{b}\|_{2}}<t\right),

we have

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\bm{F}_{n}(t)-\Phi(t)\right|\leq c\sqrt{\frac{\tau\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{a}_{i}^{4}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2}\right]^{2}}},

where
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
and
c
is some constant.
Similar results also hold for
\bm{Z}_{0}
with scale
n_{z}\times p
.

Proof.

Denote

\bm{X}_{n,i}=\frac{\bm{a}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{b}}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left(\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2}\right)}\|\bm{b}\|_{2}},

from our proof in Lemma S
3
, we have

1.

\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{X}_{n,i}\right)=0
;

2.

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{X}_{n,i}^{2}\right)=1
; and

3.

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{X}_{n,i}^{4}\right)=O_{p}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{a}_{i}^{4}\right)\right)/\mathbb{E}\left(\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}
.

Define Lyapunov coefficientss as

L_{t}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left|\bm{X}_{n,i}\right|^{t},

using Berry-Esseen Theorem with the Lyapunov coefficient
L_{4}
, we have the following result

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}|\bm{F}_{n}(t)-\Phi(t)|\leq cL_{3}\leq c\sqrt{L_{4}}

\displaystyle\implies\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}|\bm{F}_{n}(t)-\Phi(t)|\leq c\sqrt{\frac{\tau\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{a}_{i}^{4}\right)}{\mathbb{E}\left(\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}}}

for some constant
\tau
decided by the exact value of
L_{4}
.
∎

Lemma S5

(CLT for residue functional)
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
2
, assume that
\bm{\epsilon}
is a
n\times 1
random vector where each entry are i.i.d with bounded second and fourth moment, i.e.
\mathbb{E}(\bm{\epsilon}_{i}^{2})=\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\leq c_{1}
,
\mathbb{E}(\bm{\epsilon}_{i}^{4})\leq c_{2}
for some
c_{1},c_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
.
Assume a deterministic vector
\bm{a}
where
\mathbb{E}(\bm{\epsilon}_{i}^{4})\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{a}_{i}^{4}/\{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}\|\bm{a}\|^{4}\}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, we have

\frac{\left\langle\bm{a},\bm{\epsilon}\right\rangle}{\sigma_{\epsilon}\|\bm{a}\|_{2}}\to\mathcal{N}(0,1).

Proof.

Denote

\bm{X}_{n,i}=\frac{\bm{a}_{i}\bm{\epsilon}_{i}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}\|\bm{a}\|_{2}},

observe that
\bm{X}_{n,i}
s are independent for different
i
, thus we only need to check Lindeberg’s conditions before concluding our CLT results

1.

\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{X}_{n,i}\right)=0
;

2.

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{X}_{n,i}^{2}\right)=\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\epsilon}_{i}^{2}\right)\bm{a}_{i}^{2}\right\}/\left(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{2}\right)=1
; and

3.

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{X}_{n,i}^{4}\right)=\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\epsilon}_{i}^{4}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{a}_{i}^{4}\right\}/\left(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}\|\bm{a}\|^{4}\right)\to 0
as
n\to\infty
.

∎

Lemma S6

(Quantiative CLT for residue functional)
.

Under Assumption
1
, assume
\bm{a}
to be a deterministic vector, and assume that
\bm{\epsilon}
is a
n\times 1
random vector where each entry are i.i.d with bounded second and fourth moment, i.e.
\mathbb{E}(\bm{\epsilon}_{i}^{2})=\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\leq c_{1}
,
\mathbb{E}(\bm{\epsilon}_{i}^{4})\leq c_{2}
for some
c_{1},c_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
.
Let

\bm{F}_{n}(t)=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\left\langle\bm{a},\bm{\epsilon}\right\rangle}{\sigma_{\epsilon}\|\bm{a}\|_{2}}<t\right),

we have

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}|\bm{F}_{n}(t)-\Phi(t)|\leq c\sqrt{\frac{\tau\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\epsilon}_{i}^{4}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{a}_{i}^{4}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{4}}}

for some quantities
c
and
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.

Denote

\bm{X}_{n,i}=\frac{\bm{a}_{i}\bm{\epsilon}_{i}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}\|\bm{a}\|_{2}},

from our proof in Lemma S
5
, we have

1.

\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{X}_{n,i}\right)=0
;

2.

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{X}_{n,i}^{2}\right)=\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\epsilon}_{i}^{2}\right)\bm{a}_{i}^{2}\right\}/\left\{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{a}\right\}=1
; and

3.

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{X}_{n,i}^{4}\right)=\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\epsilon}_{i}^{4}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{a}_{i}^{4}\right\}/\left\{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{4}\right\}
.

Define Lyapunov coefficients as

L_{t}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left|\bm{X}_{n,i}\right|^{t},

using Berry-Esseen Theorem with the Lyapunov coefficient
L_{4}
, we have the following result

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}|\bm{F}_{n}(t)-\Phi(t)|\leq cL_{3}\leq c\sqrt{L_{4}}

\displaystyle\implies\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}|\bm{F}_{n}(t)-\Phi(t)|\leq c\sqrt{\frac{\tau\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\epsilon}_{i}^{4}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{a}_{i}^{4}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\|\bm{a}\|_{2}^{4}}}

for some constant
\tau
decided by the exact value of
L_{4}
.
∎

Lemma S7

(Von Bahr-Esseen inequality)
.

Consider
\bm{X}_{i}
s to be independent, centered random variables, i.e.
\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{X}_{i}\right)=0
for all
i
, then for
\alpha\in\left[1,2\right]
, denote
\bm{S}_{n}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{X}_{i},
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\bm{S}_{n}\right|\geq t\right)\leq nC_{\alpha}\mathbb{E}\left|\bm{X}_{i}\right|^{\alpha}t^{-\alpha},

or equivalently

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(|\bm{S}_{n}|<t\right)\geq 1-nC_{\alpha}\mathbb{E}\left|\bm{X}_{i}\right|^{\alpha}t^{-\alpha}.

Lemma S8
.

(Law of large number version of Lemma S
1
)

Under Assumption
1
, assume
\bm{X}_{0}
is a
n\times p
matrix and some deterministic vector
\bm{a}
and
\bm{b}
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{\left\langle\bm{a},\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{b}\right\rangle}{n}-\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{b}\right|\geq\epsilon\right)\leq O_{p}\left(\frac{\|\bm{a}\|^{2}_{2}\|\bm{b}\|_{2}^{2}+2\|\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{b}\|_{2}^{2}}{n\epsilon^{2}}\right).

Similar results also hold for
\bm{Z}_{0}
with scale
n_{z}\times p
.

Proof.

Notice that

\left\langle\bm{a},\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{b}\right\rangle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0_{i}}\bm{b}^{\top}\bm{x}_{0_{i}},

where
\bm{x}_{0_{i}}
is
i_{th}
i.i.d. copies of row of
\bm{X}_{0}
. We can conclude our lemma by applying Lemma S
7
.
∎

Throughout the paper, quantities related to matrices of the form
\bm{P}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{Q}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{R}
will be frequently mentioned and computed. For the clarity of our proof, we discuss its entries here in advance.

Lemma S9
.

Under Assumption
1
, for any deterministic symmetric matrix
\bm{P},\bm{Q},\bm{R}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
, we claim
\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{P}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{Q}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{R}\right)
has the
(i,j)_{th}
element as

n\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(x_{0}^{4}-3\right)(\bm{P}\mbox{diag}(\bm{Q})\bm{R})_{i,j}+(n+1)(\bm{P}\bm{Q}\bm{R})_{i,j}+{\rm Tr}(\bm{Q})(\bm{P}\bm{R})_{i,j}\right\}.

Recall that
\mbox{diag}(\bm{Q})
is a diagonal matrix obtained by dropping all off-diagonal elements of
\bm{Q}
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{P}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{Q}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{R}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=n\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(x_{0}^{4}-3\right){\rm Tr}(\bm{P}\mbox{diag}(\bm{Q})\bm{R})+(n+1){\rm Tr}(\bm{P}\bm{Q}\bm{R})\right.

\displaystyle\left.+{\rm Tr}(\bm{Q}){\rm Tr}(\bm{P}\bm{R})\right\}.

Under Assumption
1
, if the
{\rm Tr}(\bm{P}\mbox{diag}(\bm{Q})\bm{R})
is of the same order as
{\rm Tr}(\bm{P}\bm{Q}\bm{R})
, then the first term is negligible. We claim that in our discussion, the neglibility of such first term in
{\rm Tr}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{P}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{Q}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{R}\right)\right\}
applies to all of our cases.

Appendix S.4
Proof for Section
3.1

In this section, we consider the genetically predicted value
\bm{z}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{M}}
of the marginal estimator given by
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{M}}=n^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}
. Using the leave-one-out strategy, we prove Theorem
1
in three steps. We first decompose
\bm{z}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{M}}
into two parts, then provide quantitative CLT for the randomness of
\bm{\epsilon}
, treating
\bm{X}_{0}
as fixed, and finally, we provide quantitative CLT considering the randomness of
\bm{X}_{0}
, which landing in the desired second-order limit.

S.4.1
Decomposition of new prediction of marginal estimator

The general strategy for proving the CLT for
\bm{z}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{M}}
is first to decompose it into two independent parts, conditional on
\bm{X}_{0}
, and recover the Gaussian distribution using Berry-Esseen theorem
[
2
]
. Under Assumption
1
, we have

\displaystyle\bm{z}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{M}}=\frac{1}{n}\left(\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}+\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}\right).

S.4.2
Berry-Esseen bounds with the randomness of training error

Considering fixed
\bm{X}_{0}
, the only source of randomness is from the error
\bm{\epsilon}
, and we can provide the corresponding quantitative CLT using Lemma S
6
.
Denote

\bm{I}_{\text{M}p}(t)=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z}}}<t\right),

considering
\bm{X}_{0}
fixed, we can show that

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\bm{I}_{\text{M}p}(t)-\Phi_{\epsilon}(t)\right|\leq O_{p}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\epsilon}^{4}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\bm{x}_{0_{i}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z}\right)^{4}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}\left(\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z}\right)^{2}}\right),

where

\bm{x}_{0_{i}}
represents
i_{th}
row of data matrix
\bm{X}_{0}
.
Recall that

\displaystyle\bm{F}_{\text{M}}(t)=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}+\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}-n\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{z}<\sigma_{\text{M}p}\sqrt{n}t\right)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z}}}\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z}}}{\sigma_{\text{M}p}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad<\sqrt{n}t-\left.\frac{\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}-n\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{z}}{\sigma_{\text{M}p}}\right).

Let
\Lambda_{\epsilon}
be some standard Gaussian random variable depends only on the randomness of
\bm{\epsilon}
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z}}}\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z}}}{\sigma_{\text{M}p}}<\sqrt{n}t-\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\frac{\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}-n\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{z}}{\sigma_{\text{M}p}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z}}}{\sigma_{\text{M}p}}\Lambda_{\epsilon}<\sqrt{n}t-\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\frac{\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}-n\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{z}}{\sigma_{\text{M}p}}\right)\right|\leq O_{p}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\epsilon}^{4}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\bm{x}_{0_{i}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z}\right)^{4}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}\left(\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z}\right)^{2}}\right).

S.4.3
Berry-Esseen bounds with the randomness of training data matrix

Now consider the randomness of
\bm{X}_{0}
, denote

\displaystyle\bm{J}_{\text{M}p}(t)=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}-n\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}}{\sqrt{n\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(x_{0}^{4}-3\right)\sum_{i=1}^{p}(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta})_{i}^{2}(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z})_{i}^{2}+2(\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta})^{2}+\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}}}\leq t\right).

(S.1)

Using Lemma S
2
, we have following Berry-Esseen inequality

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\bm{J}_{\text{M}p}(t)-\Phi_{\bm{X}_{0}}(t)\right|\leq O_{p}(n^{-1/2}),

where
\Phi_{\bm{X}_{0}}(t)
represents the CDF of standard Gaussian depending on the randomness of
\bm{X}_{0}
. Moreover, with the randomness of
\bm{X}_{0}
, for
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}>0
,
0<\delta<1/2
, consider the subset where

\displaystyle\Omega_{0}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})\coloneqq

\displaystyle\left\{\bm{X}_{0}:\left\{\left|\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z}-n\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{z}\right|<n^{1/2+\delta}\epsilon_{1}\right\}\cap\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left\{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{x}_{0_{i}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z})^{4}-n\left(\sum_{i=j}^{p}\mathbb{E}\left(x_{0}^{4}-3\right)(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z})_{j}^{4}+3(\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{z})^{2}\right)\right|\leq n^{1/2+\delta}\epsilon_{2}\right\}\right\}.

Using the Von Bahr-Esseen bound in Lemma S
7
with
\alpha=2
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z}-n\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{z}\right|<n^{1/2+\delta}\epsilon_{1}\right)\geq 1-\frac{C}{n^{2\delta}\epsilon_{1}^{2}}

for any
\delta\text{, }\epsilon_{1}>0
.
This is equivalent to claim that by choosing
0<\delta<1/2
,
\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z}
would be of order
O_{p}(n)
with high probability.
Similarly, we can also bound the order of
\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{x}_{0_{i}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z})^{4}
using Lemma S
7

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{x}_{0_{i}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z})^{4}-n\left(\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}[x_{0}^{4}-3](\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z})_{j}^{4}+3(\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{z})^{2}\right)\right|\leq n^{1/2+\delta}\epsilon_{2}\right)\geq 1-\frac{C}{n^{2\delta}\epsilon_{2}^{2}}.

Therefore, we have shown that

\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{0}\in\Omega_{0})\geq 1-O_{p}(n^{-2\delta}),

and for
\bm{X}_{0}\in\Omega_{0}
, we have the following Berry-Esseen bound

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}X_{0}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}}{\sqrt{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\left(n\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{z}+O_{p}(n^{1/2+\delta})\right)}}<t\right)-\Phi_{\epsilon}(t)\right|\leq

\displaystyle O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n\left(\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\left(x_{0}^{4}-3\right)(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z})_{j}^{4}+3(\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{z})^{2}\right)+O_{p}(n^{1/2+\delta}p^{2})}{n^{2}(\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{z})^{2}+O_{p}(n^{3/2+\delta})}}\right)=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

Now we denote

\sigma_{\text{M}_{\bm{X}_{0}}}=\sqrt{n\left(\mathbb{E}\left(x_{0}^{4}-3\right)\sum_{i=1}^{p}(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta})_{i}^{2}(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z})_{i}^{2}+2(\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta})^{2}+\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z}\|_{2}^{2}\right)}.

Moreover, we denote
\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{0}}
the second standard Gaussian which only depends on the randomness of
\bm{X}_{0}
, then we have the following Berry-Esseen bound

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}-n\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{z}}{\sigma_{\text{M}_{\bm{X}_{0}}}}\frac{\sigma_{\text{M}_{\bm{X}_{0}}}}{\sigma_{\text{M}p}}<t\right)-\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\sigma_{\text{M}_{\bm{X}_{0}}}}{\sigma_{\text{M}p}}\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{0}}<t\right)\right|\leq O_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

It follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\frac{\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}-n\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{z}}{\sigma_{\text{M}p}}<\sqrt{n}t-\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z}}}{\sigma_{\text{M}p}}\Lambda_{\epsilon}\Bigg)\Bigg.

(S.2)

\displaystyle\left.\qquad-\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\sigma_{\text{M}_{\bm{X}_{0}}}}{\sigma_{\text{M}p}}\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{0}}<\sqrt{n}t-\frac{\sqrt{n\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{z}}}{\sigma_{\text{M}p}}\Lambda_{\epsilon}\right)\right|\leq O_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

Combining
section

S.4.2
and
eq.

S.2
above, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\bm{F}_{\text{M}}(t)-\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\sigma_{\text{M}_{\bm{X}_{0}}}}{\sigma_{\text{M}p}}\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{0}}+\frac{\sqrt{n\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{z}}}{\sigma_{\text{M}p}}\Lambda_{\epsilon}<\sqrt{n}t\right)\right|\leq O_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

For
\bm{X}_{0}\in\Omega_{0}
, we have shown that

O_{p}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\epsilon}^{4}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\bm{x}_{0_{i}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z}\right)^{4}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}\left(\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{z}\right)^{2}}\right)=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

Using the convolution formula for independent Gaussian variables, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\sigma_{\text{M}_{\bm{X}_{0}}}}{\sigma_{\text{M}p}}\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{0}}+\frac{\sqrt{n\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{z}}}{\sigma_{\text{M}p}}\Lambda_{\epsilon}<\sqrt{n}t\right)=\Phi(t).

It follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\bm{F}_{\text{M}}(t)-\Phi(t)\right|\leq O_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

Appendix S.5
Proof for Section
3.2

To obtain quantitative CLTs for
A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}})
,
we will need first to establish the quantitative CLTs of the quadratic form
\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}
.
In this section, we prove Theorem
2
using martingale CLT.
Due to the sparsity structure of
\bm{\beta}
, we only consider the first
m
non-zero entries of
\bm{\beta}
and treat them as i.i.d. random variables.
That is, we have
\mathbb{E}(\bm{\beta}_{i})=0
,
\mathbb{E}(\bm{\beta}_{i}^{2})=\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}/p
for
1\leq i\leq m
and
\bm{\beta}_{1},\bm{\beta}_{2},\cdots\bm{\beta}_{m}
are i.i.d. random variables.
We proceed by applying the martingale CLT to show that
\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}
converges to Gaussian as
m\to\infty
, and use Berry-Esseen type bound to quantify the Kolmorogrov distance between CDF of the quadratic form
\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}
and that of a standard Gaussian random variable.

Let
\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}
be the leading principle minor of
\bm{\Sigma}
by removing the last
p-m
rows and columns of matrix
\bm{\Sigma}
. Let
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}
be the strictly upper-triangular nilpotent matrix by setting all diagonal elements and lower-triangular elements of
\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}
to be zero.
By applying Proposition S
1
, all eigenvalues of
\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}
would be of order
\Theta_{p}(1)
. Moreover, since the last
p-m
entries of
\bm{\beta}
consist of
0
, we drop those terms by only considering the truncated
\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}
, where we drop zero entries of
\bm{\beta}
.
We aim to prove the following theorem for general symmetric statistics.

Theorem S3

(Berry-Esseen bound for quadratic form)
.

Adopt Assumption
2
, and consider a general symmetric, positive semi-definite matrix
\bm{\Sigma}
with
c\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}\prec\bm{\Sigma}\prec C\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}
for some
0<c<C
. We use
\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}
to denote the truncated matrix produced by removing the last
p-m
rows and columns of
\bm{\Sigma}
, we have the following quantitative CLT for symmetric quantity
\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{p\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}-\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m})}{\sqrt{p^{2}\left\{\mathbb{E}({\bm{\beta}}^{4})-3\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}/p^{2}\right\}\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{(\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m})}_{i,i}}^{2}+2\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}{\rm Tr}\left\{(\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m})^{2}\right\}}}<t\right)-\Phi(t)\right|

\displaystyle\leq O_{p}(m^{-1/5}).

Remark S1
.

We may drop the assumption that
\bm{\Sigma}
is positive definite. Notice that
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}
is always positive semi-definite in Lemma S
10
.

Before proving Theorem S
3
, we use Propositions S
1
-S
2
to bound the change of eigenvalues concerning matrices minor and matrices summation.

Proposition S1

(Eigenvalue interlacing theorem)
.

Suppose
\bm{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
a symmetric matrix and denote
\bm{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}
with
m<n
as the principal minor of matrix
\bm{A}
. If
A
has eigenvalues
\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{n}
and
\bm{B}
has eigenvalues
\theta_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\theta_{m}
, then we have

\lambda_{k+n-m}\leq\theta_{k}\leq\lambda_{k}

for
k\in[1,m]
.

Proposition S2

(Weyl’s inequality)
.

For
m\times m
symmetric matrices
\bm{A}
and
\bm{B}
, and use
\lambda_{i}(\cdot)
to represent the
i_{th}
largest eigenvalue of the matrix, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}(\bm{A})+\lambda_{m}(\bm{B})\leq\lambda_{i}(\bm{A}+\bm{B})\leq\lambda_{i}(\bm{A})+\lambda_{1}(\bm{B}).

Remark S2
.

The eigenvalue interlacing theorem will be applied to show that truncating the covariance matrix
\bm{\Sigma}
to its leading principles would not change the order of eigenvalues, whereas Weyl’s inequality helps provide an upper bound for a key quantity in determining the convergence rate.

By Proposition S
1
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{n-m+1}(\bm{\Sigma})\leq\lambda_{1}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime})\leq\lambda_{1}(\bm{\Sigma})\quad\mbox{and}\quad

\displaystyle\lambda_{n}(\bm{\Sigma})\leq\lambda_{m}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime})\leq\lambda_{m}(\bm{\Sigma}).

This means that every eigenvalue of
\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}
is of order
\Theta_{p}(1)
. Now we quantify the eigenvalues of the key quantities.

Lemma S10
.

Adopt Assumption
1
, let
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}
be the strictly upper-triangular nilpotent matrix by setting all diagonal elements and lower-triangular elements of
\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}
to be zero, then we can conclude that there exists some
\psi_{1}\geq 0,\psi_{2}\geq 0
, such that

\psi_{1}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\preceq\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\preceq\psi_{2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}.

Proof.

Notice
\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}=diag(\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime})+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}
. From the fact that
c\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}\prec\bm{\Sigma}\prec C\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}
, every diagonal element of matrix
\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}
is of order one. By applying Lemma S
2
, we have
\lambda_{i}(\bm{\Sigma})=\lambda_{i}(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}})+\Theta_{p}(1)
. This means that the eigenvalues of
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}
are also of order one.
By applying eigenvalue decomposition, it follows that all eigenvalues of
(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}})^{2}
are bounded. Expanding this term, we have

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}})^{2}=\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}+(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2})^{\top}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}.

Therefore, for any constant
i\in[1,m]
, by Lemma S
2
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}\left\{\left(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\right)^{2}\right\}=\lambda_{i}\left\{\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}+\left(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}\right)^{\top}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}\right\}\implies

(S.1)

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\right)+\lambda_{m}\left\{\left(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}\right)^{\top}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}\right\}\leq\lambda_{i}\left(\left(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\right)^{2}\right)\leq\lambda_{i}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\right)+\lambda_{1}\left\{(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2})^{\top}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}\right\}.

(S.2)

Note that we only need to work on the upper bound for
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}
, because it is the summation of two positive semi-definite matrices and we have
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\succeq 0
. Since
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}
is a strictly upper-triangular matrix,
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}
is also strictly upper-triangular.
Therefore, all its eigenvalues would be zero. This means that
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}
is a positive semi-definite matrix, and so is
(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2})^{\top}
. Therefore, we conclude that
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2}+(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{2})^{\top}\succeq 0
. By plugging this back to
eq.

S.2
, we have

\lambda_{i}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\right)\leq\lambda_{i}\left\{\left(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\right)^{2}\right\}=O_{p}(1).

Similarly,
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}
and
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}
are both symmetric and positive semi-definite. By Weyl’s inequality, for
\forall i\in[1,m]
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\right)+\lambda_{m}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{i}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\right)\leq\lambda_{i}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\right)+\lambda_{1}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\right)

\displaystyle\implies 0\leq\lambda_{i}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{i}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\right)=O_{p}(1).

∎

Remark S3
.

The reason why we construct and examine the eigenvalues of
(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}+\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}})^{2}
is that the Weyl’s inequality holds only when the matrix is symmetric.
Our overall strategy is to extract information of eigenvalues from
{\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}}^{2}
, link it to
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}
, and then guarantee the positive semi-definiteness of each component to obtain an upper bound.

Now we are ready to prove Theorem S
3
.

Proof of Theorem S
3
.

Considering the following filtration

\mathfrak{F}_{t}=\left.\sigma\left(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}_{i}}\right|1\leq i\leq t\right).

We can construct corresponding martingale difference sequence

\displaystyle\xi_{t}=2p\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{i,t}\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{i}\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{t}+p\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t,t}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{t}^{2}-\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{p}\right)

for
1\leq t\leq m
. It is trivial to check that

1.

\xi_{t}
is adapted to the filtration
\mathfrak{F_{t}}
;

2.

\mathbb{E}\left(\xi_{t}|\mathfrak{F}_{t-1}\right)=0
;

3.

T_{m}=\sum_{t=1}^{m}\xi_{t}=p\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}-\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime})
; and

4.

\mbox{var}\left(T_{m}\right)=p^{2}\left\{\mathbb{E}(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{4})-3\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}/p^{2}\right\}\sum_{i=1}^{m}{\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{i,i}}^{2}+2\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}{\rm Tr}({\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}}^{2})=\Theta_{p}(m)
.

Denote the conditional variance
S_{m}=\mbox{var}(T_{m})^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\left(\xi_{t}^{2}|\mathfrak{F}_{t-1}\right)
, we aim to check
\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m}-1\right|^{2}\to 0
as
m\to\infty
, which leads to
S_{m}\overset{p}{\to}1
in martingale CLT.
We also aim to quantify the convergence behavior in
L_{2}
to provide the Berry-Esseen bound for the convergence rate.

We start by decomposing the
\mathbb{E}\left(\xi_{t}^{2}|\mathfrak{F}_{t-1}\right)
into three parts: an uncentered part, a centered part, and a constant. Notice that

\displaystyle\left.S_{m}=\frac{p^{2}}{\mbox{var}(T_{m})}\sum_{t=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\left\{4\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{t}^{2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{i,t}\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{i}\right)^{2}+4\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\bm{\Sigma}_{i,t}\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{i}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t,t}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{t}^{2}-\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{p}\right)+{\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}}_{t,t}^{2}\left({\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}}_{t}^{2}-\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{p}\right)^{2}\right|\mathfrak{F}_{t-1}\right\}.

By conditioning on
\mathfrak{F}_{t-1}
, the first part is an uncentered random variable, the second part is a centered random variable, and the third part is a constant. After the conditional expectation, the only term with randomness is of the form

\upsilon_{m}(t)=2\sqrt{p}\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{i,t}\bm{\beta}_{i}.

Denote the following two constants

D_{1}=\mathbb{E}\left\{p^{3/2}\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{t}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{t}^{2}-\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{p}\right)\right\}\quad\mbox{and}\quad D_{2}=\mathbb{E}\left\{p^{2}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{t}^{2}-\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{p}\right)^{2}\right\}.

Note that
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
are constants of order
\Theta_{p}(1)
. Rearrange these terms, we have

\displaystyle S_{m}=\frac{p^{2}}{\mbox{var}(T_{m})}\sum_{t=1}^{m}\left(\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\upsilon_{m}(t)^{2}+2D_{1}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t,t}\upsilon_{m}(t)+D_{2}{\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}}_{t,t}^{2}\right).

It is easy to check that
\mathbb{E}(S_{m})=1
. Again, notice that the second term has a mean of zero and the third term is a constant, we have the following inequality

\displaystyle\left|S_{m}-1\right|\leq C\mbox{var}(T_{m})^{-1}\left\{\left|\sum_{t=1}^{m}\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\upsilon_{m}(t)^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left(\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\upsilon_{m}(t)^{2}\right)\right|+\left|\sum_{t=1}^{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t,t}\upsilon_{m}(t)\right|\right\}.

For the first term, we further split it into “off-diagonal” and “on-diagonal” parts, subtracted by their corresponding means

\displaystyle|S_{m}-1|\leq C\mbox{var}(T_{m})^{-1}\left\{p\left|\sum_{t=1}^{m}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq t-1}\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{i}\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{j}\right|+p\left|\sum_{t=1}^{m}\sum_{1\leq k\leq t-1}{\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{k,t}}^{2}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{k}^{2}-\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{p}\right)\right|+\left|\sum_{t=1}^{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t,t}\upsilon_{m}(t)\right|\right\}.

For clarity, we denote

S_{m,1}=p\left|\sum_{t=1}^{m}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq t-1}\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{i}\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{j}\right|,\quad S_{m,2}=p\left|\sum_{t=1}^{m}\sum_{1\leq k\leq t-1}{\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{k,t}}^{2}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{k}^{2}-\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{p}\right)\right|,

and

\quad S_{m,3}=\left|\sum_{t=1}^{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t,t}\upsilon_{m}(t)\right|.

Intuitively, we try to isolate the contribution from the diagonal elements of
\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}
from the contribution of the off-diagonal terms.
Such inequality immediately leads to the following inequality

\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m}-1\right|^{2}\leq C\mbox{var}(T_{m})^{-2}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m,1}\right|^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m,2}\right|^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m,3}\right|^{2}\right\}.

Now we will carefully examine the upper bounds of
\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m,1}\right|^{2}
,
\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m,2}\right|^{2}
, and
\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m,3}\right|^{2}
. For
\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m,1}\right|^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m,1}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=p^{2}\sum_{t_{1}=1}^{m}\sum_{t_{2}=1}^{m}\sum_{1\leq s_{1}<s_{2}\leq t_{1}-1}\sum_{1\leq s_{3}<s_{4}\leq t_{2}-1}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t_{1},s_{1}}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t_{1},s_{2}}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t_{2},s_{3}}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t_{2},s_{4}}\bm{\beta}_{s_{1}}\bm{\beta}_{s_{2}}\bm{\beta}_{s_{3}}\bm{\beta}_{s_{4}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{t_{1},t_{2}=1}^{m}\sum_{1\leq s_{1}<s_{2}<\min(t_{1},t_{2})}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t_{1},s_{1}}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t_{1},s_{2}}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t_{2},s_{1}}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t_{2},s_{2}}.

Here
\min(t_{1},t_{2})
is inspired by the fact that we need to choose
s_{1}=s_{3},s_{2}=s_{4}
to have a nonzero expectation. Note that
s_{1}\leq t_{1}-1
and
s_{3}\leq t_{2}-1
, so we require
s_{1},s_{2}<\min(t_{1},t_{2})
. The same idea applies wherever
\min(t_{1},t_{2})
appears. Moreover, for
\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m,2}\right|^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m,2}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle=p^{2}\mathbb{E}\left\{\sum_{t_{1},t_{2}=1}^{m}\sum_{1\leq k<\min(t_{1},t_{2})}{\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t,t_{1}}}^{2}{\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{k,t_{2}}}^{2}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{k}^{2}-\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{p}\right)^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{t_{1},t_{2}=1}^{m}\sum_{1\leq s_{1},s_{2}<\min(t_{1},t_{2})}{\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{k,t_{1}}}^{2}{\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{k,t_{2}}}^{2}.

It follows that

\mathbb{E}\left(\left|S_{m,1}^{2}\right|+\left|S_{m,2}^{2}\right|\right)\leq C\sum_{t_{1}=1}^{m}\sum_{t_{2}=1}^{m}\sum_{1\leq s_{1},s_{2}<\min(t_{1},t_{2})}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t_{1},s_{1}}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t_{1},s_{2}}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t_{2},s_{1}}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t_{2},s_{2}}=C\|\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\|_{F}^{2},

where
\|\cdot\|_{F}
is the Frobenius norm.
In addition, for
\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m,3}\right|^{2}
, recall that
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}
is the matrix obtained from
\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}
by dropping the lower-triangular part and setting all diagonal terms equal to
0
. Then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m,3}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq Cp\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{t_{1},t_{2}}^{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t_{1},t_{1}}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t_{2},t_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{t_{1}-1}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{i,t_{1}}\widetilde{\bm{\beta}_{i}}\sum_{j=1}^{t_{2}-1}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{j,t_{2}}\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sum_{t_{1}=1}^{m}\sum_{t_{2}=1}^{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t_{1},t_{1}}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t_{2},t_{2}}\sum_{1\leq i<\min(t_{1},t_{2})}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{i,t_{1}}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{i,t_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left\{\sum_{t_{1}=1}^{m}\sum_{t_{2}=1}^{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t_{1},t_{1}}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{t_{2},t_{2}}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\right)_{t_{1},t_{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\langle\bm{w},\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\bm{w}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\|\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\|_{2}\|\bm{w}\|_{2}^{2}=\|\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\|_{2}O_{p}(m),

where we use vector
\bm{w}
to denote the spectrum of
\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}
.
Now we show that
\psi_{1}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\preceq\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\preceq\psi_{2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}
for some constant
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\geq 0
. This will immediately implies that
\|\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\|_{2}=\Theta_{p}(1)
.
To do this, we first show that the principle minor is bounded using the eigenvalue interlacing theorem in Proposition S
1
, then the boundness of
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}
follows from Weyl’s inequality.
Adopting Assumption
1
and by Proposition S
1
, we have that every eigenvalue of
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}
is of order
\Theta_{p}(1)
.
Notice that
\|\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\|_{2}=\lambda_{1}(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}})=O_{p}(1)
and
\|\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\|_{F}={\rm Tr}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}\right)^{1/2}\leq O_{p}(m^{1/2})
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m,1}\right|^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m,2}\right|^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m,3}\right|^{2}\leq O_{p}(m)\implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m}-1\right|^{2}\leq C\mbox{var}(T_{m})^{-2}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m,1}\right|^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m,2}\right|^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m,3}\right|^{2}\right\}\leq O_{p}(m^{-1}).

By the Berry-Esseen bound proven in
[
28
]
, the following inequality holds

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\frac{p\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}-\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime})}{\mbox{var}(T_{m})^{1/2}}\right|\leq C_{p}\left(\mathbb{E}\left|S_{m}-1\right|^{2}+\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\left|\xi_{i}\right|^{4}}{\mbox{var}(T_{m})^{2}}\right)^{-1/5}.

Notice that
\mathbb{E}\left|\xi_{i}\right|^{4}=O_{p}(1)
for
\forall i\in[1,m]
. Moreover, we have

\mbox{var}(T_{m})=p^{2}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{4}\right)-3\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}}{p^{2}}\right\}\sum_{i=1}^{m}{\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{i,i}}^{2}+2\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}}^{2}\right)=\Theta_{p}(m).

This leads to the following quantitative CLT

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{p\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}-\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}{\rm Tr}\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}\right)}{\sqrt{p^{2}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{4}\right)-3\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}/p^{2}\right\}\sum_{i=1}^{m}{\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}_{i,i}}^{2}+2\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}{\rm Tr}\left({\bm{\Sigma}^{\prime}}^{2}\right)}}<t\right)-\Phi(t)\right|\leq O_{p}(m^{-1/5}).

We can then translate it to the following notation

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{p\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}-\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}{\rm Tr}\left(\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\right)}{\sqrt{p^{2}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left({\bm{\beta}}^{4}\right)-3\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}/p^{2}\right\}\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\left(\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\right)}_{i,i}}^{2}+2\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}{\rm Tr}\left\{\left(\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\right)^{2}\right\}}}<t\right)-\Phi(t)\right|

\displaystyle\leq O_{p}(m^{-1/5}).

∎

The next lemma shows that if features are isotropic, we do not need to use the martingale CLT. Instead, we can approach the second-order limit using the Berry-Esseen theorem, which provides a better Berry-Esseen bound.

Lemma S11

(Berry-Esseen bounds for quadratic grom with isotropic features)
.

Adopt Assumption
2
and when
\bm{\Sigma}=\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}
, consider quadratic quantity
\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\beta}
, we have the following quantitative CLT

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{p\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\beta}-m\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{\sqrt{mp^{2}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\beta}^{4}\right)-3\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}/p^{2}\right\}+2m\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}}}<t\right)-\Phi(t)\right|\leq O(m^{-1/2}).

Proof.

Notice that we can decompose
\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\beta}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\bm{\beta}_{i}^{2}
, where
\bm{\beta}_{i}^{2}
are i.i.d. items.
Consider

\xi_{m,i}=\frac{\bm{\beta}_{i}^{2}}{\sqrt{m\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\beta}^{4}\right)-3\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}/p^{2}\right\}+2m\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}/p^{2}}}.

It is easy to check

1.

\mathbb{E}\left(\xi_{m,i}\right)=0
;

2.

\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\left(\xi_{m,i}^{2}\right)=1
; and

3.

\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\left(\xi_{m,i}^{4}\right)\leq O_{p}(m^{-1})
.

Recall Lyapunov coefficients are defined as

L_{t}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left|X_{n,i}\right|^{t}.

Using the Berry-Esseen Theorem
[
2
]
with the Lyapunov coefficient
L_{4}
, we have the following result

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\xi_{i}<t\right)-\Phi(t)\right|\leq CL_{3}\leq C\sqrt{L_{4}}

\displaystyle\implies\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\xi_{i}<t\right)-\Phi(t)\right|\leq O_{p}(m^{-1/2})

for some constant
\tau
decided by the exact value of
L_{4}
. With proper rescaling, we have our desired Berry-Esseen inequality.
∎

Appendix S.6
Proof for Section
3.3

To prove the quantitative CLT of
A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{M}})
in Theorem
3
, we use eight steps with a leave-one-out technique. Briefly, we first decompose the numerator of
A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{M}})
into two parts and then provide the quantitative CLT regarding the randomness of
\bm{\epsilon}_{z}
. Next, we provide the quantitative CLT regarding the randomness of
\bm{Z}_{0}
,
\bm{\epsilon}
,
\bm{X}_{0}
, and
\bm{\beta}
, along with first-order concentrations. This concludes the CLT for the numerator part in
A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{M}})
. Similarly, we obtain the first-order limit of the denominator part in
A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{M}})
, and then we conclude the CLT of
A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{M}})
by applying Slutsky’s theorem.

S.6.1
Numerator decomposition

Notice that we cannot decompose the numerator into the summation of i.i.d. random variables. Therefore, we need to consider multiple quantitative CLTs to establish our Theorem
3
. The numerator can be rewritten as

\displaystyle(\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{Z}^{\top}+\bm{\epsilon}_{z}^{\top})\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon})=\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon})+\bm{\epsilon}_{z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon}).

The advantage of such decomposition is that the first quantity does not involve
\bm{\epsilon}_{z}
. Therefore, we can consider the first quantity fixed, while we can use Lemma S
6
to provide quantitative CLT for the second quantity.

S.6.2
Berry-Esseen bounds with the randomness of testing error

Conditional on
\bm{Z}_{0},\bm{X}_{0},\bm{\epsilon},\bm{\beta}
, and considering the randomness of
\bm{\epsilon}_{z}
, then by using Lemma S
5
, we obtain the following Berry-Esseen inequality

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{\epsilon}_{z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}\left(\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon}\right)}{\sqrt{\sigma_{\epsilon_{z}}^{2}\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}}}<t\right)-\Phi_{\epsilon_{z}}(t)\right|\leq O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n_{z}}\left\{\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}\left(\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon}\right)_{i}^{4}\right\}}{\|\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}\left(\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon}\right)\|_{2}^{4}}}\right).

(S.1)

Denote

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}=

\displaystyle n_{z}n^{2}\left[\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}n_{z}}{n}\left(2\gamma_{2}^{2}+\gamma_{1}\gamma_{3}\right)+\frac{n_{z}}{n}\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\gamma_{1}+2\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}\gamma_{2}^{2}+\left(\sigma_{\epsilon_{z}}^{2}+\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\gamma_{1}\right)\left\{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\left(\gamma_{3}+\omega_{2}\gamma_{1}\frac{p}{n}\right)+\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\omega_{2}\frac{p}{n}\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+n_{z}\left\{\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\beta}^{4}\right)-3\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}}{p^{2}}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\right)_{i,i}^{2}+\frac{2\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}}{p^{2}}{\rm Tr}\left\{\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\right)^{2}\right\}\right\}\right].

Consider the following CDF

\displaystyle\bm{H}_{\text{M}}(t)=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon})+\bm{\epsilon}_{z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon})-nn_{z}\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\gamma_{2}}{\sigma}<t\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{\epsilon}_{z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon})}{\sqrt{\sigma_{\epsilon_{z}}^{2}\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}}}\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{\epsilon_{z}}^{2}\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}}}{\sigma}<t-\frac{\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon})-nn_{z}\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\gamma_{2}}{\sigma}\right).

regarding the randomness of
\bm{\epsilon}_{z}
, and by the Berry-Esseen bound we have in
eq.

S.1
, denoted by
\Lambda_{\epsilon_{z}}
, the standard Gaussian variable depending only on the randomness of
\bm{\epsilon}_{z}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\bm{H}_{\text{M}}(t)-\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{\epsilon_{z}}^{2}\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}}}{\sigma}\Lambda_{\epsilon_{z}}<t-\frac{\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon})-nn_{z}\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\gamma_{2}}{\sigma}\right)\right|

(S.2)

\displaystyle\leq O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n_{z}}\left\{\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}\left(\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon}\right)_{i}^{4}\right\}}{\|\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}\left(\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon}\right)\|_{2}^{4}}}\right).

S.6.3
Berry-Esseen bounds with the randomness of testing data matrix

Conditional on
\bm{\beta}
,
\bm{X}_{0}
and
\epsilon
, we now consider the randomness of
\bm{Z}_{0}
. By applying Lemma S
1
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon})-n_{z}\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon})}{\sigma_{1}}\leq t\right)-\Phi_{\bm{Z}_{0}}(t)\right|\leq O_{p}(n_{z}^{-1/2}),

(S.3)

where

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}^{2}=n_{z}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(z_{0}^{4}-3\right)\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}\right)_{i}^{2}\left\{\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}^{\top}\left(\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\epsilon\right)\right\}_{i}^{2}+2\left\{\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{X}^{\top}\left(\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon}\right)\right\}^{2}+\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}^{\top}\left(\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon}\right)\|_{2}^{2}\right\}.

Note that
\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}=\sum_{i}^{n_{z}}\|\bm{z}_{0_{i}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\|_{2}^{2}
.
Using Lemma S
7
, for
\forall\delta\in\left(0,1/2\right)
and
\forall\epsilon_{1}>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}-n_{z}\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\right|<n_{z}^{1/2+\delta}n^{2}\kappa_{1}\epsilon_{1}\right)\geq 1-\frac{C}{\epsilon_{1}^{2}n_{z}^{2\delta}}.

Moreover, we have
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{Z}_{0}}\left(\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\right)=O_{p}(n_{z}n^{2}\kappa_{1})

and

\mathbb{E}_{\bm{X}_{0}}\left(\|\bm{z}_{0_{i}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\|_{2}^{4}\right)=\mathbb{E}\left(z_{0}^{4}-3\right)\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\right)_{j}^{4}+3\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\|_{2}^{4}=O_{p}(n^{4}\kappa_{1}^{2}).

Similarly, note that
\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\bm{z}_{0}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\right)^{8}\right\}=O_{p}(n^{8}\kappa_{1}^{4})
. For
\forall\delta\in\left(0,1/2\right)
and
\forall\epsilon_{2}>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{z}}\left(\bm{z}_{0_{i}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\right)^{4}-n_{z}\mathbb{E}\left\{(\bm{z}_{0}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y})^{4}\right\}\right|<n_{z}^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}n^{4}\kappa_{1}^{2}\epsilon_{2}\right)\geq 1-\frac{C}{\epsilon_{2}^{2}n_{z}^{2\delta}}.

Consider the randomness of
\bm{Z}_{0}
, we define the subset
\Omega_{1}
as following:

\displaystyle\Omega_{1}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})\coloneqq

\displaystyle\Bigg\{\bm{Z}_{0}:\left\{\left|\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}-n_{z}\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\right|<n_{z}^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}n^{2}\kappa_{1}\epsilon_{1}\right\}\cap

\displaystyle\qquad\left\{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{z}}(\bm{z}_{0_{i}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y})^{4}-n_{z}\mathbb{E}\left\{(\bm{z}_{0}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y})^{4}\right\}\right|<n_{z}^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}n^{4}\kappa_{1}^{2}\epsilon_{2}\right\}\Bigg\}

for some
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}>0
. Notice that we have already shown that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\bm{Z}_{0}\in\Omega_{1})\geq 1-O_{p}(n_{z}^{-2\delta})

for
\forall\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}>0
.

Notice that if we rescale
\bm{\beta}
to have a length of one, the higher moments of its entries cannot guarantee a smaller order. This hinders us from directly deriving its concentration using Markov’s inequality. Alternatively, some random matrix tricks have been used to prove the desired concentrations.
Proposition S
3
is originally proposed in
[
4
]
and is refined by
[
61
]
. Our Proposition S
4
adopts the refined version. Lemma S
12
is designed to obtain the negligibility of higher moment terms in
\sigma_{1}^{2}
. It helps us eliminate all higher moment terms in our later analysis.

Proposition S3
.

(Originally proposed in
[
4
]
)

Let
\bm{x}=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})^{\top}
be i.i.d. standardized entries and
\bm{A}
be an
n\times n
matrix. For any
p\geq 2
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\bm{x}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{x}-{\rm Tr}(\bm{A})\right|^{p}\leq K_{p}\left[\left\{\mathbb{E}|x_{1}|^{4}{\rm Tr}(\bm{A}\bm{A}^{\top})\right\}^{p/2}+\mathbb{E}|x_{1}|^{2p}{\rm Tr}(\bm{A}\bm{A}^{\top})^{p/2}\right].

Remark S4
.

The original version proposed in Lemma 2.7 of
[
4
]
is more general, as it also includes the matrices with complex entries. We only consider the real matrix in our setting for the simplicity of the usage.

Proposition S4
.

(Originally proposed in
[
61
]
)

For a random vector
\bm{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
with standardized entries, assume that
\bm{a}\sim\bm{F}(\bm{0},\bm{\Sigma}_{a})
, where
\bm{\Sigma}_{a}
is positive and semi-definite, and a deterministic positive semi-definite matrix
\bm{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
where
{\rm Tr}(\bm{A}\bm{\Sigma}_{a})\neq 0
.
Moreover, if
\mathbb{E}\left(|a_{i}|^{4}\right)\leq\upsilon_{4}
, then we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{a}-{\rm Tr}(\bm{A}\bm{\Sigma}_{a})}{{\rm Tr}(\bm{A}\bm{\Sigma}_{a})}\right|\geq\epsilon\right)\leq\mbox{var}\left(\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{a}}{{\rm Tr}(\bm{A}\bm{\Sigma}_{a})}\right)/\epsilon^{2},

where

\mbox{var}\left(\frac{\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{a}}{{\rm Tr}(\bm{A}\bm{\Sigma}_{a})}\right)\leq 2\cdot C_{2}\cdot\upsilon_{4}\cdot\frac{{\rm Tr}(\bm{A}\bm{\Sigma}_{a}\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{a})}{{\rm Tr}(\bm{A}\bm{\Sigma}_{a})^{2}}.

Lemma S12
.

Under Assumption
1
, for
\forall\delta\in(0,1/2)
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta})_{i}^{2}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}(\bm{\Sigma}_{i,j}^{1/2})^{2}\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{p}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m_{j,j}}\right|\geq\epsilon p^{-1/2+\delta}\right)\leq O_{p}(p^{-2\delta-1}).

Notice that
\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}/p\bm{\Sigma}_{i,i}=O_{p}(p^{-1})
.

Proof.

Using Chebyshev’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}\right)_{i}^{2}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left(\bm{\Sigma}_{i,j}^{1/2}\right)^{2}\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{p}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m_{j,j}}\right|>\epsilon p^{-1/2+\delta}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left\{\left|\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}\right)_{i}^{2}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left(\bm{\Sigma}_{i,j}^{1/2}\right)^{2}\bm{\beta}_{j}^{2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m_{j,j}}\right|^{2}\right\}}{\epsilon^{2}p^{-1+2\delta}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M}{\epsilon^{2}p^{-1+2\delta}}\left[\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}\right)_{i}^{4}\right\}+\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}}{p^{2}}\bm{\Sigma}_{i,i}^{2}\right].

Notice that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}\right)_{i}^{4}\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\beta}^{4}-\frac{3\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}}{p^{2}}\right)\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}_{i,j}\right)^{4}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m_{j,j}}+\frac{3\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}}{p^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left\{\left(\bm{\Sigma}_{i,j}^{1/2}\right)^{2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m_{j,j}}\right\}\sum_{k=1}^{p}\left\{\left(\bm{\Sigma}_{i,k}^{1/2}\right)^{2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m_{k,k}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mathbb{\mathbb{}}E\left(\bm{\beta}^{4}-\frac{3\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}}{p^{2}}\right)\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}_{i,j}\right)^{4}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m_{j,j}}+\frac{3\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}}{p^{2}}\bm{\Sigma}_{i,i}^{2}.

Therefore, we have

\mathbb{E}\left\{\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}\right)_{i}^{4}\right\}=O_{p}(p^{-2})
,
which implies

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}\right)_{i}^{2}-\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left(\bm{\Sigma}_{i,j}^{1/2}\right)^{2}\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m_{j,j}}\right|>\epsilon p^{-1/2+\delta}\right)\leq O_{p}(p^{-1-2\delta}).

∎

Lemma S13
.

Following Proposition S
4
, for
\forall\delta\in(0,1/2)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{p}{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Psi}\bm{\beta}-{\rm Tr}\left(\bm{\Psi}\mathbb{I}_{m}\right)\right|<p^{\delta+1/2}\epsilon_{1}\right)\geq 1-O_{p}\left(p^{-1-2\delta}{\rm Tr}\left(\bm{\Psi}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Psi}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\right)\right)\geq 1-O_{p}(p^{-2\delta}).

for any deterministic positive semi-definite matrix
\bm{\Psi}
with bounded eigenvalues.

By Boole’s inequality, for a countable set of events
A_{1},A_{2},\cdots
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{\infty}A_{i}\right)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(A_{i}).

Then using Lemma S
12
and this union bound,
we have
