\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{p}{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}-{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m})\right|<O_{p}(p^{-\delta})\right)\geq 1-O_{p}\left(p^{2\delta}{\rm Tr}\left\{(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{A}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m})^{2}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\implies\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}-\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{p}{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m})\right|<O_{p}(p^{-1-\delta})\right)\geq 1-O_{p}(p^{-1+2\delta}),\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{p}{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}-{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m})\right|<O_{p}(p^{-1-\delta})\right)\geq 1-O_{p}\left(p^{2+2\delta}{\rm Tr}\left\{(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m})^{2}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\implies\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{p}{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m})\right|<O_{p}(p^{-2-\delta})\right)\geq 1-O_{p}(p^{-1+2\delta}).

For
\forall\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{3}>0
and
\delta\in(0,1/2)
, we define the following subset where quantities related to
\bm{\beta}
concentrate properly

\displaystyle\Xi_{5}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},

\displaystyle\epsilon_{3})\coloneqq\left\{\bm{\beta}:\left\{\left|\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}-\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\gamma_{1}\right|<p^{\delta-1/2}\epsilon_{1}\right\}\cap\right.\left\{\left|\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}-\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\left.\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{p}{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m})\right|<p^{-2-\delta}\epsilon_{2}\right\}\cap\left\{\left|\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}-\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}{p}{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m})\right|<p^{-1-\delta}\epsilon_{3}\right\}\right\}.

We have shown that
\mathbb{P}(\bm{\beta}\in\Xi_{5})\geq 1-O_{p}(p^{-1+2\delta})
. We introduce the following notations for simplicity

1.

\widetilde{\kappa}_{1}={\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma})
;

2.

\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}={\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2})
;

3.

\widetilde{\kappa}_{3}={\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma})
;

4.

\widetilde{\kappa}_{4}={\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m})
;

5.

\widetilde{\kappa}_{5}={\rm Tr}\left\{(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m})^{2}\right\}
; and

6.

\widetilde{\kappa}_{6}={\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m})
.

Notice that for
\bm{\beta}\in\Xi_{5}
, we can further simplify our Berry-Esseen upper bounds in
eq.

S.10
as follows

\displaystyle O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n_{z}\left\{n^{2}\left(n\widetilde{\omega}_{1}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}+n^{2}\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}^{2}\right\}+O_{p}(p^{1/2+\delta})}{n_{z}^{2}\left\{n^{2}\left(n\widetilde{\omega}_{1}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}+n^{2}\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}^{2}\right\}+O_{p}(p^{3/2+\delta})}}\right)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n_{z}\left\{n^{2}\left(n\widetilde{\kappa}_{4}\right)^{2}+n^{2}\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}^{2}\right\}+O_{p}(p^{1/2+\delta})}{n_{z}^{2}\left\{n^{2}\left(n\widetilde{\kappa}_{4}\right)^{2}+n^{2}\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}^{2}\right\}+O_{p}(p^{3/2+\delta})}}\right)\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(x_{0}^{4}-3\right)\sum_{i=1}^{p}(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta})_{i}^{4}+3\widetilde{\omega}_{1}^{2}\right\}+O_{p}(n^{1/2+\delta}n_{w}^{-4})}{n^{2}\widetilde{\omega}_{2}^{2}+O_{p}(n^{3/2+\delta}n_{w}^{-4})}}\right)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{3n\left(\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}/p\widetilde{\kappa}_{4}\right)^{2}+O_{p}(n^{1/2+\delta}n_{w}^{-4})}{n^{2}\left(\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}/p\widetilde{\kappa}_{4}\right)^{2}+O_{p}(n^{3/2+\delta}n_{w}^{-4})}}\right).

(S.12)

Now we use Theorem S
3
to obtain the quantitative CLT for the quadratic quantities. Theorem S
3
holds because we have
\psi_{1}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}\preceq n\left(\bm{W}^{\top}\bm{W}+n_{w}\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}\right)^{-1}\preceq\psi_{2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}
, under the condition
n_{w}\asymp n
in Assumption
4
.
Denote

\displaystyle\sigma_{5}^{2}=\left(\mathbb{E}\left({\bm{\beta}}^{4}\right)-3\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}}{p^{2}}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{p}{{\left(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\right)}_{i,i}}^{2}+\frac{2\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}}{p^{2}}\widetilde{\kappa}_{5}.

(S.13)

By Theorem S
3
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{P}\left(nn_{z}\frac{\widetilde{\omega}_{3}-\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}/p\widetilde{\kappa}_{6}}{\sigma_{5}}\frac{\sigma_{5}}{\hbar}<t-\frac{n_{z}\sigma_{3}}{\hbar}\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{0}}-\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.nn_{z}\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}/p\widetilde{\kappa}_{6}-\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}/(\lambda n_{w}m_{w}(-\lambda)^{2})\left(m_{w}(-\lambda)\gamma_{1}+\xi_{1}-m/p\right)}{\hbar}-\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\frac{n_{z}\sqrt{n\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\widetilde{\omega}_{2}+O_{p}(p^{\delta-3/2})}}{\hbar}\Lambda_{\epsilon}-\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{\epsilon_{z}}^{2}n_{z}\left\{n\left\{n\widetilde{\omega}_{1}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}+n\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\right\}+O_{p}(p^{1/2+\delta})}}{\hbar}\Lambda_{\epsilon_{z}}-\right.\bigr.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\bigl.\left.\frac{\sqrt{2n_{z}n^{2}\widetilde{\omega}_{3}^{2}+n_{z}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\left\{n\left\{n\widetilde{\omega}_{2}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\left(\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}+\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\right)\right\}\right\}}}{\hbar}\Lambda_{\bm{Z}_{0}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\mathbb{P}\left(nn_{z}\frac{\sigma_{5}}{\hbar}\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}<t-\frac{n_{z}\sqrt{n\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}/p^{2}\left\{2\widetilde{\kappa}_{6}^{2}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{4}{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m})\right\}+O_{p}(p^{\delta-3/2})}}{\hbar}\Lambda_{\bm{X}_{0}}-\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.nn_{z}\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}/p\widetilde{\kappa}_{6}-\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}/(\lambda n_{w}m_{w}(-\lambda)^{2})\left(m_{w}(-\lambda)\gamma_{1}+\xi_{1}-m/p\right)}{\hbar}-\frac{n_{z}\sqrt{n\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}/p\widetilde{\kappa}_{4}+O_{p}(p^{\delta-3/2})}}{\hbar}\Lambda_{\epsilon}\right.\bigr.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\bigl.-\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{\epsilon_{z}}^{2}n_{z}\left\{n\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}/p\left\{n\widetilde{\kappa}_{4}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m})\right\}+n\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}\right\}+O_{p}(p^{1/2+\delta})}}{\hbar}\Lambda_{\epsilon_{z}}\right.\bigr.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\bigl.\left.-\frac{\sqrt{2n_{z}n^{2}\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}/p^{2}\widetilde{\kappa}_{6}^{2}+n_{z}\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\gamma_{1}\left[n\left\{n\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}/p\widetilde{\kappa}_{6}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}(\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\gamma_{1}+\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2})\right\}\right]}}{\hbar}\Lambda_{\bm{Z}_{0}}\right)\right|\leq O_{p}(m^{-1/5}).

(S.14)

S.8.7
Concentration using anisotropic local law with the randomness of reference panel

In this section, we provide the first-order limit of quantities related to
\bm{W}_{0}
using the anisotropic local law
[
35
]
. Let
\bm{e}_{i}
be a
p\times 1
vector with an entry of
1
on
i_{th}
row.
Notice that
|\bm{e}_{i}|_{2}=1
and we have
\sum_{i=1}^{m}\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{e}_{i}={\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m})
. We will use this relationship to isolate each diagonal element of the desired matrices and apply the anisotropic local law to obtain their first-order limits. By summation and applying the union bound, we will obtain the concentration over the desired trace. Note that we can drop the matrix
\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}
by simply summing up to
m
. This property is convenient as it allows us to avoid most of the
\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}
when applying the anisotropic local law. However, some quantities of
\sigma_{5}^{2}
in
eq.

S.13
still require more careful analysis due to their special structure.
Specifically, there are four quantities in
section

S.8.6
that require further analysis, including

1.

{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma})
;

2.

{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2})
;

3.

{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma})
; and

4.

{\rm Tr}\left\{(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m})^{2}\right\}
.

The matrix
\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}
does not cause trouble for the first three quantities as we only sum up to
m
terms. However, the last quantity requires more non-trivial analysis, as we cannot avoid the matrix
\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}
in its complicated structure. This makes the analysis much different from that in
eq.

S.10
, and we will illustrate this point later.

For the first quantity
{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma})
, similar to our argument in Lemma S
14
, for some small
\vartheta>0
and a sufficiently large
D
, the following inequality holds with probability at least
1-O_{p}(p^{-D})

\left|-\lambda\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{e}_{i}+\frac{1}{n_{w}}\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+m_{w}\left(-\lambda)\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}^{2}\bm{e}_{i}\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(\frac{p^{\vartheta}}{n_{w}}).

Therefore, by union bound, with probability of at least
1-O_{p}(p^{-D+1})
, we have

\displaystyle\left|{\rm Tr}\left(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\right)-{\rm Tr}\left\{(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+m_{w}(-\lambda)\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}^{2}\right\}\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(p^{\vartheta})\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\left|{\rm Tr}\left(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\right)-{\rm Tr}\left\{(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+m_{w}(-\lambda)\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}^{2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\right\}\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(p^{\vartheta}).

For the second quantity
{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2})
and third quantity
{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma})
, notice that they differ only by two fixed matrices
\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}
with bounded eigenvalues. Then by similar argument in
eq.

S.10
, we can conclude that, with probability of at least
1-O_{p}(p^{-D})
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{e}_{i}-\frac{m_{w}^{\prime}(-\lambda)}{m_{w}(-\lambda)^{2}}\frac{1}{n_{w}^{2}\lambda^{2}}e_{i}^{\top}(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+m_{w}(-\lambda)\bm{\Sigma})^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}^{2}\bm{e}_{i}\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(p^{\vartheta-2})\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\left|\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{e}_{i}-\frac{m_{w}^{\prime}(-\lambda)}{m_{w}(-\lambda)^{2}}\frac{1}{n_{w}^{2}\lambda^{2}}\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+m_{w}(-\lambda)\bm{\Sigma})^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}^{3}\bm{e}_{i}\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(p^{\vartheta-2}).

It follows that

\displaystyle\left|{\rm Tr}\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right)-\frac{m_{w}^{\prime}(-\lambda)}{m_{w}(-\lambda)^{2}}\frac{1}{n_{w}^{2}\lambda^{2}}{\rm Tr}\left\{(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+m_{w}(-\lambda)\bm{\Sigma})^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}^{2}\right\}\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(p^{\vartheta-1}),

\displaystyle\left|{\rm Tr}\left(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\right)-\frac{m_{w}^{\prime}(-\lambda)}{m_{w}(-\lambda)^{2}}\frac{1}{n_{w}^{2}\lambda^{2}}{\rm Tr}\left\{(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+m_{w}(-\lambda)\bm{\Sigma})^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}^{3}\right\}\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(p^{\vartheta-1}),

\displaystyle\left|{\rm Tr}\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\right)-\frac{m_{w}^{\prime}(-\lambda)}{m_{w}(-\lambda)^{2}}\frac{1}{n_{w}^{2}\lambda^{2}}{\rm Tr}\left\{(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+m_{w}(-\lambda)\bm{\Sigma})^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}^{2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\right\}\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(p^{\vartheta-1}),

and

\displaystyle\left|{\rm Tr}\left(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\right)-\frac{m_{w}^{\prime}(-\lambda)}{m_{w}(-\lambda)^{2}}\frac{1}{n_{w}^{2}\lambda^{2}}{\rm Tr}\left\{(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+m_{w}(-\lambda)\bm{\Sigma})^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}^{3}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\right\}\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(p^{\vartheta-1}),

with probability of at least
1-O_{p}(p^{-D+1})
.

Now we consider the last quantity
{\rm Tr}\left\{(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m})^{2}\right\}
.
Note that

{\rm Tr}\left\{(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m})^{2}\right\}={\rm Tr}\left(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{e}_{i}.

We have

\displaystyle\left.\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\left(\frac{\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{W}_{0}^{\top}\bm{W}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}}{n_{w}}+\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}\right)^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}\left(\frac{\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{W}_{0}^{\top}\bm{W}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}}{n_{w}}+\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}\right)^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{e}_{i}\right.

\displaystyle\left.=\frac{d\left(\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\left\{\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}\left(n_{w}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{W}_{0}^{\top}\bm{W}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}+\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}\right)\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}-\tau\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\right\}^{-1}\bm{e}_{i}\right)}{d\tau}\right|_{\tau=0}.

As mentioned previously, we cannot avoid the matrix
\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}
here. Therefore, we match
\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}
with the derivative of some quantity. Similar to our proof of Lemma S
14
, we have

\displaystyle\vec{\underline{\bm{v}}}=\vec{\underline{\bm{w}}}=\left[\begin{array}[]{c}(\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}-\tau/\lambda\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{e}_{i}\\
\hline\cr[2pt/2pt]0\end{array}\right].

We denote the Stieltjes transform of the asymptotic eigenvalue distribution of

\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}-\tau/\lambda\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}

as
m_{w}(\lambda,\tau)
. Here we are not able to establish an explicit linear relationship between eigenvalues of
(\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}-\tau/\lambda\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2})^{-1}
and eigenvalues of
\bm{\Sigma}
. Therefore, we approach

\left.dm_{w}(\lambda,\tau)/d\tau\right|_{\tau=0}

through implicit function and eigenvalue perturbation formula. Specifically,
m_{w}(\lambda,\tau)
satisfies following equality

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{w}(\lambda,\tau)}=-\lambda+\frac{\phi_{w}}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\pi_{i}^{\prime}}{1+\pi_{i}^{\prime}m_{w}(\lambda,\tau)},

(S.15)

where
\pi_{i}^{\prime}
denotes the
i_{th}
eigenvalue of
(\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}-\tau/\lambda\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2})^{-1}
,
i=1,\ldots,p
.
It is worth mentioning that both
m_{w}(\lambda,\tau)
and
\pi_{i}^{\prime}
depend on
\tau
.
Now taking the derivative with respect to
\tau
on both sides of
eq.

S.15
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\phi_{w}}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}

\displaystyle\frac{d\pi_{i}^{\prime}}{d\tau}\left\{\frac{1}{1+\pi_{i}^{\prime}m_{w}(\lambda,\tau)}-\frac{m_{w}(\lambda,\tau)\pi_{i}^{\prime}}{(1+\pi_{i}^{\prime}m_{w}(\lambda,\tau))^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle=-\frac{dm_{w}(\lambda,\tau)}{d\tau}\left\{\frac{1}{m_{w}(\lambda,\tau)^{2}}-\frac{\phi_{w}}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\pi_{i}^{\prime 2}}{(1+\pi_{i}^{\prime}m_{w}(\lambda,\tau))^{2}}\right\}.

Note that
\left.m_{w}(\lambda,\tau)\right|_{\tau=0}=m_{w}(-\lambda)
and
\left.\pi_{i}=\pi_{i}^{\prime}\right|_{\tau=0}
. It follows that

\displaystyle\frac{\phi_{w}}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left.\frac{d\pi_{i}^{\prime}}{d\tau}\right|_{\tau=0}\frac{1}{(1+m_{w}(-\lambda)\pi_{i})^{2}}=-\left.\frac{dm_{w}(\lambda,\tau)}{d\tau}\right|_{\tau=0}\frac{1}{m_{w}^{\prime}(-\lambda)}.

Therefore, as our target is
\left.d\pi_{i}^{\prime}/d\tau\right|_{\tau=0}
, which represents how the eigenvalues of
\bm{\Sigma}
change after a small perturbation (though such perturbation is non-linear due to the inverse), we can quantify this change using the formula for eigenvalue perturbation
[
31
]

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d\pi_{i}^{\prime}}{d\tau}\right|_{\tau=0}

\displaystyle=\bm{u}_{i}^{\top}\left.\frac{d(\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}-\tau/\lambda\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2})^{-1}}{d\tau}\right|_{\tau=0}\bm{u}_{i}

\displaystyle=\left.\bm{u}_{i}^{\top}\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}-\tau/\lambda\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right)^{-1}\lambda^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}-\tau/\lambda\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right)^{-1}\bm{u}_{i}\right|_{\tau=0}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{-1}\bm{u}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{3/2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{3/2}\bm{u}_{i}=\pi_{i}^{3}\lambda^{-1}\left\langle\bm{u}_{i},\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{u}_{i}\right\rangle,

where
\bm{u}_{i}
represents the
i_{th}
eigenvector of
\bm{\Sigma}
. It follows that

\displaystyle\left.\frac{dm_{w}(\lambda,\tau)}{d\tau}\right|_{\tau=0}=-\frac{\phi_{w}}{\lambda p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{m_{w}(-\lambda)^{\prime}\pi_{i}^{3}}{(1+\pi_{i}m_{w}(-\lambda))^{2}}\left\langle u_{i},\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}u_{i}\right\rangle.

Let

\displaystyle\bm{\Pi}(-\lambda)=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}-\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}-\tau/\lambda\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}+m_{w}(\lambda,\tau)\right)^{-1}&0\\
0&m_{w}(\lambda,\tau)\end{array}\right],

with probability of at least
1-O_{p}(p^{-D})
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\langle\vec{\underline{\bm{v}}},\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}(\bm{G}(-\lambda)-\bm{\Pi}(-\lambda))\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}\vec{\underline{\bm{w}}}\rangle\right|\prec\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(1),

where

\bm{G}(-\lambda)=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}-(\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}-\tau/\lambda\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2})&\frac{\bm{W}_{0}^{\top}}{\sqrt{n_{w}}}\\
\frac{\bm{W}_{0}}{\sqrt{n_{w}}}&\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{n_{w}}\end{array}\right]^{-1}

and

\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}-(\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}-\tau/\lambda\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2})^{-1}&0\\
0&\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{n_{w}}\end{array}\right].

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\left|\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\left\{\left(\frac{\bm{W}_{0}^{\top}\bm{W}_{0}}{\lambda n_{w}}+\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}-\frac{\tau}{\lambda}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right)^{-1}-\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}-\frac{\tau}{\lambda}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}+m_{w}(\lambda,\tau)\right)^{-1}\right\}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{e}_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\prec\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(1).

Then we have the following inequality

\displaystyle\left|\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\left(\frac{\bm{W}_{0}^{\top}\bm{W}_{0}}{n_{w}}+\lambda\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}-\tau\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right)^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{e}_{i}-\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\frac{1}{\lambda}\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}-\frac{\tau}{\lambda}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}+m_{w}(\lambda,\tau)\right)^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{e}_{i}\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(p^{\vartheta-1}).

Taking the derivative with respect to
\tau
and set
\tau=0
, we have

\displaystyle\Bigg|\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\left(\frac{\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{W}_{0}^{\top}\bm{W}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}}{n_{w}}+\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}\right)^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}\left(\frac{\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{W}_{0}^{\top}\bm{W}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}}{n_{w}}+\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}\right)^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{e}_{i}-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{w}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\left(\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}-\lambda\frac{dm_{w}(\lambda,\tau)}{d\tau}\right)(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{w}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{e}_{i}\Bigg|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\Bigg|\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\left(\frac{\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{W}_{0}^{\top}\bm{W}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}}{n_{w}}+\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}\right)^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}\left(\frac{\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{W}_{0}^{\top}\bm{W}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}}{n_{w}}+\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}\right)^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{e}_{i}-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{w}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\left(\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}+\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{\prime}\phi_{w}\mathfrak{n}(\bm{\Sigma},m)\right)(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{w}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{e}_{i}\Bigg|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(1),

where

\mathfrak{n}(\bm{\Sigma},m)=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\phi_{w}\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{\prime}\pi_{i}^{3}}{(1+\pi_{i}\mathfrak{m}_{w})^{2}}\left\langle u_{i},\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}u_{i}\right\rangle.

Now we denote

\mathcal{M}=\bm{\Sigma}(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{w}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\left(\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}+\mathfrak{n}(\bm{\Sigma},m)\right)(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{w}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}.

After proper rescaling, we may conclude that, with probability of at least
1-O_{p}(p^{-D})
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{e}_{i}-\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}n_{w}^{2}}\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathcal{M}\bm{e}_{i}\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(p^{\vartheta-2})

for some small enough
\vartheta>0
.

Combining with our result on
\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{e}_{i}
, using union bound, we can show that, with probability of at least
1-O_{p}(p^{-D+1})
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\sigma_{5}^{2}-\left\{\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}n_{w}^{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\beta}^{4}\right)-\frac{3\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}}{p^{2}}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left\{\left(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{w}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}^{2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\right\}_{i,i}^{2}+\frac{2\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{4}}{\lambda^{2}n_{w}^{2}p^{2}}{\rm Tr}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(p^{\vartheta-3}).

Our concentration inequalities over the randomness of
\bm{W}_{0}
also help us simplify the Berry-Esseen bounds in
section

S.8.6
as follows

\displaystyle O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n_{z}\left\{n^{2}\left(n\widetilde{\kappa}_{4}\right)^{2}+n^{2}\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}^{2}\right\}+O_{p}(p^{1/2+\delta})}{n_{z}^{2}\left\{n^{2}\left(n\widetilde{\kappa}_{4}\right)^{2}+n^{2}\widetilde{\kappa}_{2}^{2}\right\}+O_{p}(p^{3/2+\delta})}}\right)=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{3n\left(\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}/p\widetilde{\kappa}_{4}\right)^{2}+O_{p}(n^{1/2+\delta}n_{w}^{-4})}{n^{2}\left(\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}/p\widetilde{\kappa}_{4}\right)^{2}+O_{p}(n^{3/2+\delta}n_{w}^{-4})}}\right)=O_{p}(m^{-1/2})

Combining
eq.

S.3
,
eq.

S.5
,
eq.

S.7
,
section

S.8.5
, and
section

S.8.6
, the worst Berry-Esseen upper bound is of order
O_{p}(m^{-1/5})
. By combining all those independent Gaussian random variables and using the convolution formula for independent Gaussian variables, we obtain the following quantitative CLT for the numerator

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\bm{H}_{\text{W}}(t)-\Phi(t)\right|\leq O_{p}(m^{-1/5}),

(S.16)

with probability of at least
1-O_{p}(p^{-D+1})
over the randomness of
\bm{W}_{0}
.

S.8.8
Limits of the denominator

In this section, we provide the limits of the quantity
\|\widehat{\bm{y}_{z}}\|
in the denominator. The following lemma provides us with the desired concentration.

Lemma S16
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
4
,
with probability of at least
1-O_{p}(p^{-D})
over the randomness of
\bm{W}_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}-\left(\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{2}}\right)\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}nn_{z}}{\lambda^{2}n_{w}^{2}}\left\{\frac{n}{\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{3}}\left(3\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}+\mathfrak{m}_{w}\gamma_{1}-\frac{2m}{p}\right)\right.\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\left.\qquad+\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{2}}\left(\gamma_{1}+\frac{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}{\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}}\right)\left(1-2\pi_{1}+\pi_{2}\right)p\right\}\right|<O_{p}(p^{1/2+\delta})\right)\geq 1-O_{p}(p^{-2\delta}).

This concentration has been proven sequentially as we approach the quantitative CLT of the numerator of
A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{W}}(\lambda))
. Briefly, it involves
\sigma_{1}^{2}
from
eq.

S.2
. We can conduct the same sequential conditioning and restrict our random variables to the same subsets where quantities concentrate well, as done for the numerator analysis. In addition, as we have shown in
eq.

S.13
, with even much looser assumptions, we still have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\|\bm{Z}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon}_{z}\|_{2}^{2}-n_{z}(\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\gamma_{1}+\sigma_{\epsilon_{z}}^{2})\right|<O_{p}(n_{z}^{1/2+\delta})\right)\geq 1-O_{p}(p^{-2\delta}).

S.8.9
Major quantitative CLT

Combining Lemma S
16
and
eq.

S.16
, we obtain the following Berry-Esseen inequality as our major conclusion.

Theorem S6
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
4
, as
p\to\infty
, with probability of at least
1-O_{p}(p^{-D})
for some large enough
D\in\mathbb{R}
over the randomness of
\bm{W}_{0}
, the following Berry-Esseen bound holds for
A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{W}}(\beta))

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{\eta_{\text{W}}n_{z}}\left\{\frac{(\bm{Z}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon}_{z})^{\top}\bm{Z}(\bm{W}^{\top}\bm{W}+n_{w}\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}}{\|\bm{Z}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon}_{z}\|_{2}\|\bm{Z}(\bm{W}^{\top}\bm{W}+n_{w}\lambda\mathbb{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\|_{2}}-\widetilde{A}_{\text{W}}\right\}<t\right)-\Phi(t)\right|\leq O_{p}(m^{-1/5}).

The
\widetilde{A}_{\text{W}}
and
\eta_{\text{W}}
are defined in Theorem
5
.
We explicitly write out our previous definition on
\eta_{\text{W}}
as

\eta_{\text{W}}=\eta_{\text{Wn}}/(\eta_{\text{W{d1}}}+\eta_{\text{W{d2}}}+\eta_{\text{W{d3}}})
, where

\displaystyle\eta_{\text{Wn}}=\left\{n/\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{3}\left(3\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}+\mathfrak{m}_{w}\gamma_{1}-2m/p\right)+p/\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{2}(\gamma_{1}/h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2})\left(1-2\pi_{1}+\pi_{2}\right)\right\}\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\gamma_{1}/h_{\bm{\beta}_{z}}^{2},

\displaystyle\eta_{\text{Wd1}}=1/\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{3}\left(3\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}+\mathfrak{m}_{w}\gamma_{1}-2m/p\right)\left(n\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\gamma_{1}/h_{\bm{\beta}_{z}}^{2}+n_{z}\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\gamma_{1}/h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\right),

\displaystyle\eta_{\text{Wd2}}=p/(\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{2})(1-2\pi_{1}+\pi_{2})(\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\gamma_{1}/h_{\bm{\beta}_{z}}^{2})\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}\gamma_{1}/h_{\bm{\beta}}^{2},

\displaystyle\eta_{\text{Wd3}}=2(n+n_{z})/\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{4}(\mathfrak{m}_{w}\gamma_{1}+\xi_{1}-m/p)^{2}\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2},\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\eta_{\text{Wd4}}=nn_{z}\Bigg\{\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\beta}^{4}\right)/\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}-3\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}/p^{2}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left\{\left(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{w}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}^{2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\right\}_{i,i}^{2}\bigr.

\displaystyle\bigl.+2\sigma_{\bm{\beta}}^{2}/p^{2}{\rm Tr}\left\{\left((\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{w}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}^{2}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\right)^{2}\right\}\Bigg\}.

Theorem S
6
leads to the Theorem
5
in Section
4.2
.

S.8.10
Additional results for isotropic features

In this section, we will present more details for the CLT of
A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{W}}(\lambda))
when
\bm{\Sigma}=\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}
.

Fact1:
When
\bm{\Sigma}=\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}
,
eq.

4.1
has a closed-form solution for
\mathfrak{m}_{w}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{w}=\frac{\sqrt{(\lambda+\phi_{w}-1)^{2}+4\lambda}-(\lambda+\phi_{w}-1)}{2\lambda}.

Moreover, we have

\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{\prime}=\left(\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{2}}-\phi_{w}\frac{1}{\left(1+\mathfrak{m}_{w}\right)^{2}}\right)^{-1}

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{r}_{w}=\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{2}}{\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{\prime}}=1-\phi_{w}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{w}}{1+\mathfrak{m}_{w}}\right)^{2}=1-4\phi_{w}\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{(\lambda+\phi_{w}-1)^{2}+4\lambda}+(\lambda+\phi_{w}+1)}\right\}^{2}.

Fact2:
Another interesting quantity appears in
\eta_{\text{W}}
is

\mathfrak{n}(\bm{\Sigma},m)=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\phi_{w}\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{\prime}\pi_{i}^{3}}{(1+\pi_{i}\mathfrak{m}_{w})^{2}}\langle\bm{u}_{i},\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{m}\bm{u}_{i}\rangle.

Notice that when
\bm{\Sigma}=\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}
, all eigenvalues
\pi_{i}=1
and eigenvectors
\bm{u}_{i}=\bm{e}_{i}
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{n}(\bm{\Sigma}=\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p},m)=\frac{m}{p}\frac{\phi_{w}\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{\prime}}{(1+\mathfrak{m}_{w})^{2}}.

Note that

\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{\prime}}{(1+\mathfrak{m}_{w})^{2}}=\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{2}}\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{2}}{(1+\mathfrak{m}_{w})^{2}}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{w}^{\prime}}{(1+\mathfrak{m}_{w})^{2}}=\frac{1}{\left\{\sqrt{(\lambda+\phi_{w}-1)^{2}+4\lambda}+(\lambda+\phi_{w}+1)\right\}^{2}/4-\phi_{w}}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{n}(\bm{\Sigma}=\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p},m)=\frac{\phi_{w}m}{p}\frac{1}{\left\{\sqrt{(\lambda+\phi_{w}-1)^{2}+4\lambda}+(\lambda+\phi_{w}+1)\right\}^{2}/4-\phi_{w}}.

Appendix S.9
Proof for Section
S.2.1

In this section, we consider the genetically predicted value
\bm{z}^{T}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{R}}(\lambda)
of the traditional ridge estimator given by
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{R}}(\lambda)=(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda)^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}
. Our previous argument using the leave-one-out strategy no longer works because it is difficult to obtain the second-order limit of quantities such as
\bm{z}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{\beta}
. Instead of working with the generic distribution
\mathcal{F}
, we use the asymptotic normal approximation of de-biased estimators under Gaussian assumptions on the data proposed by
[
8
]
.
It takes three steps to establish the quantitative CLT for
\bm{z}^{T}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{R}}(\lambda)
under Gaussian assumptions. First, we adopt the framework of the asymptotic distribution of the de-biased estimator to quantify the quantitative CLT. Then, we provide the first-order concentration of two key items,
\widehat{df}
and
\widehat{V}(\theta)
, whose explicit definitions are provided in later sections. For simplicity, we denote

\bm{R}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X},-n\lambda)=(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1},

and we will write it as
\bm{R}
in Sections
S.9
and
S.10
.

S.9.1
Asymptotic distribution of the de-biased ridge estimator

To be consistent with
[
8
]
, we estimate confidence interval of fixed quantity
\theta=\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\bm{z}\|_{2}\langle\bm{z},\bm{\beta}\rangle
, where the
\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\bm{z}\|_{2}
part is added for normalization. We first introduce
\widehat{V}(\theta)
to quantify the variation of
\langle\bm{z},\widehat{\bm{\beta}}\rangle

\widehat{V}(\theta)=\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\bm{z}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{y}-\bm{X}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}\|_{2}^{2}+{\rm Tr}\left((\widehat{\bm{H}}-\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{2}\right)\left(\langle\bm{z},\widehat{\bm{\beta}}\rangle-\langle\bm{z},\bm{\beta}\rangle\right)^{2},

where

\widehat{\bm{H}}=\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}.

Then under Assumptions
1
-
2
and S
1
, as
p\to\infty
, we have

\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\langle\bm{z},\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(de-bias)}\rangle-\langle\bm{z},\bm{\beta}\rangle}{\widehat{V}(\theta)^{1/2}/(n-\widehat{df})}<t\right)-\Phi(t)\right|\right\}\to 0,

where

\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(de-bias)}=\widehat{\bm{\beta}}+(n-\widehat{df})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{y}-\bm{X}\widehat{\bm{\beta}})

and

\widehat{df}={\rm Tr}\left\{\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\right\}.

Here
\widehat{df}
is the effective degrees-of-freedom and
\widehat{H}
stands for the gradient of the map
\bm{y}\mapsto\bm{X}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}
.

S.9.2
First-order concentration of
\widehat{df}

Now we provide the limit of
\widehat{df}
, and we can decompose this term into two parts

\displaystyle\widehat{df}={\rm Tr}\{\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\}=p-n\lambda{\rm Tr}\{(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\}.

Note that

{\rm Tr}\{(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}\}^{-1})=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{e}_{i}.

The following lemma helps us provide concentration for each summand.

Lemma S17
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
2
and S
1
, for some large enough
D\in\mathbb{R}
, small
\vartheta>0
and
\eta\gg p^{-1}
, with probability of at least
1-O(p^{-D})
over the randomness of
\bm{X}_{0}
, we have

\left|-\lambda\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\left(n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right)^{-1}\bm{e}_{i}+n^{-1}\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-1}\bm{e}_{i}\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(\frac{p^{\vartheta}}{n}),

where

\psi(\lambda)=\sqrt{\frac{Im(\mathfrak{m}_{n})}{n\eta}}+\frac{1}{n\eta}.

Proof.

Let

\bm{R}_{n}(-\lambda)=\left(\frac{\bm{X}_{0}}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{\Sigma}\frac{\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}}{\sqrt{n}}+\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{n}\right)^{-1},\quad\bm{R}_{p}(-\lambda)=\left(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\frac{\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\bm{X}_{0}}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}+\lambda\bm{I}_{p}\right)^{-1},

\bm{G}(-\lambda)=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}-\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}&\frac{\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}}{\sqrt{n}}\\
\frac{\bm{X}_{0}}{\sqrt{n}}&\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{n}\end{array}\right]^{-1},\quad\bm{\Pi}(-\lambda)=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}-\bm{\Sigma}(1+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}&0\\
0&\mathfrak{m}_{n}\end{array}\right],\quad\text{and}\quad\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\bm{\Sigma}&0\\
0&\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{n}\end{array}\right].

By Proposition S
5
, we further have

\displaystyle\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}&0\\
0&\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{n}\end{array}\right]\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle G(-\lambda)=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}(-\lambda n)\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}(\lambda n\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}&B\\
C&\frac{1}{\lambda}\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{n}-\frac{1}{n\lambda^{2}}\bm{X}_{0}(\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}+\frac{1}{n}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0})^{-1}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\end{array}\right].

Denote

\underline{\vec{\bm{v}}}=\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\vec{\bm{e}_{i}}\\
\hline\cr[2pt/2pt]0\end{array}\right]\quad\text{and}\quad\vec{\underline{\bm{w}}}=\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\vec{\bm{e}_{i}}\\
\hline\cr 0\end{array}\right],

which are of shape
(n+p)\times 1
.
From the Theorem 3.7 of
[
35
]
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\langle\vec{\underline{\bm{v}}},\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}(\bm{G}(-\lambda)-\bm{\Pi}(-\lambda))\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}\vec{\underline{\bm{w}}}\rangle\right|\prec\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(1).

It follows that

\displaystyle\left|\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\left\{(-\lambda n)\bm{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\lambda n\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1/2}+(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}\right\}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{e}_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\prec\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(1).

Rearranging terms, we have

\displaystyle\left|-\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}(n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2})^{-1}\bm{e}_{i}+\frac{1}{\lambda n}\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{e}_{i}\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}\left(\frac{p^{\vartheta}}{\lambda n}\right).

∎

From Lemma S
17
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1}^{p}\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}(n\lambda\mathbb{I}_{p}+\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2})^{-1}\bm{e}_{i}-\frac{1}{\lambda n}{\rm Tr}\left((\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\right)\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(\frac{p^{1+\vartheta}}{\lambda n}).

(S.1)

Now by choosing
D>1
and applying union bound, with probability of at least
1-O(p^{-D+1})
, we have

\displaystyle\left|{\rm Tr}\left((\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{I}_{p})^{-1}\right)-\frac{1}{\lambda n}{\rm Tr}\left((\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\right)\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(\frac{p^{1+\vartheta}}{\lambda n})

Note that

(\lambda n)^{-1}{\rm Tr}\left((\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\right)=O_{p}(1).

This means that
{\rm Tr}\left((\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\right)
concentrates around
(\lambda n)^{-1}{\rm Tr}\left((\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\right)
with high probability. It follows that

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{df}}{p-{\rm Tr}\left((\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\right)}\overset{p}{\to}1.

(S.2)

S.9.3
First-order concentration of
\widehat{V}(\theta)

To analyze the first-order concentration of
\widehat{V}(\theta)
, we can break this down into finding the concentrations of the four different quantities below.

Task 1:

We provide the first order concentration of
{\rm Tr}\left((\widehat{\bm{H}}-\mathbb{I}_{p})^{2}\right)
.

Notice that

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}(\widehat{\bm{H}}^{2})-2{\rm Tr}(\widehat{\bm{H}})+p

\displaystyle={\rm Tr}\left\{\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\right\}

\displaystyle-2{\rm Tr}\left\{\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\right\}+p,

which implies

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}\left((\widehat{\bm{H}}-\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{2}\right)=

\displaystyle n^{2}\lambda^{2}{\rm Tr}\left((\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-2}\right)=n^{2}\lambda^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-2}\bm{e}_{i}.

Therefore, our question boils down to find the concentration of

\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{I}_{p})^{-2}\bm{e}_{i}.

Denote

\displaystyle\bm{G}(-\lambda+\tau)=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}-\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}&\frac{\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}}{\sqrt{n}}\\
\frac{\bm{X}_{0}}{\sqrt{n}}&(\lambda-\tau)\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{n}\end{array}\right]^{-1},

\displaystyle\bm{\Pi}(-\lambda+\tau)=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}-\bm{\Sigma}(\mathbb{I}_{p}+m_{n}(-\lambda+\tau)\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}&0\\
0&m_{n}(-\lambda+\tau)\end{array}\right],\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\bm{\Sigma}&0\\
0&\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{n}\end{array}\right].

By applying Proposition S
5
, we can explicitly compute
\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}
and
\bm{G}(-\lambda+\tau)
as follows

\displaystyle\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}&0\\
0&\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{n}\end{array}\right]\quad\text{and}\quad\bm{G}(-\lambda+\tau)=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\left(-\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}-\frac{\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}}{(\lambda-\tau)n}\right)^{-1}&\bm{B}\\
\bm{C}&\bm{D}\end{array}\right],

where
\bm{B}
,
\bm{C}
, and
\bm{D}
are some matrices with shapes of
p\times n
,
n\times p
, and
n\times n
, respectively.
Denote

\underline{\vec{\bm{v}}}=\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\vec{\bm{e}_{i}}\\
\hline\cr[2pt/2pt]0\end{array}\right]\quad\text{and}\quad\vec{\underline{\bm{w}}}=\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\vec{\bm{e}_{i}}\\
\hline\cr 0\end{array}\right],

both of which are of shape
(n+p)\times 1
.
By the anisotropic local law
[
35
]
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\langle\vec{\underline{\bm{v}}},\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}(\bm{G}(-\lambda)-\bm{\Pi}(-\lambda))\underline{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}\vec{\underline{\bm{w}}}\rangle\right|\prec\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(1).

After simplification, for some small
\vartheta>0
, with probability of at least
1-O_{p}(p^{-D})
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{I}_{p}+m_{n}(-\lambda+\tau)\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-1}\bm{e}_{i}-\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{I}_{p}+\frac{\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}}{n(\lambda-\tau)}\right)^{-1}\bm{e}_{i}\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(p^{\vartheta})

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\lambda-\tau}\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{I}_{p}+m_{n}(-\lambda+\tau)\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-1}\bm{e}_{i}-\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\left((\lambda-\tau)\mathbb{I}_{p}+\frac{\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}}{n}\right)^{-1}\bm{e}_{i}\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(p^{\vartheta}).

Notice that
m_{n}(-\lambda+\tau)
satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{n}(-\lambda+\tau)}=\lambda-\tau+\phi_{n}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\pi_{i}}{1+m_{n}(-\lambda+\tau)\pi_{i}},

from which we have

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d(m_{n}(-\lambda+\tau))}{d\tau}\right|_{\tau=0}=\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}.

Taking the derivative with respect to
\tau
and set
\tau=0
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\left\{\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-1}\bm{e}_{i}-\lambda\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}_{n}e_{i}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{e}_{i}\right\}-e_{i}^{\top}\left(\lambda\mathbb{I}_{p}+\frac{\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}}{n}\right)^{-2}\bm{e}_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(p^{\vartheta})

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n^{2}\lambda^{2}}\left\{\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-1}\bm{e}_{i}-\lambda\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}_{n}\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{e}_{i}\right\}-\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\left(n\lambda\mathbb{I}_{p}+\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\right)^{-2}\bm{e}_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}\left(\frac{p^{\vartheta}}{n^{2}}\right).

Since it holds for
\forall i\in[1,p]
and our choice of
D
is arbitrary, we can always choose
D>1
. Then by union bound, with probability of at least
1-O_{p}(p^{1-D})
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n^{2}\lambda^{2}}\left\{{\rm Tr}\left(\left(\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-1}\right)-\lambda\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}{\rm Tr}\left(\left(\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\right)\right\}-{\rm Tr}\left(\left(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+\lambda n\mathbb{I}_{p}\right)^{-2}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(\frac{p^{1+\vartheta}}{n^{2}}).

Therefore, we omit terms with smaller order since
{\rm Tr}\left(\left(\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-2}\right)\succ O_{p}(p^{\vartheta})
.

Task 2:
We now provide the first-order concentration of
\left(\langle\bm{z},\widehat{\bm{\beta}}\rangle-\langle\bm{z},\bm{\beta}\rangle\right)^{2}
.
From the definition, we have

\displaystyle\left\{\bm{z}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}-\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\beta}\right\}^{2}=\left\{\bm{z}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\epsilon-n\lambda\bm{z}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{\beta}\right\}^{2}.

We have shown that, with probability of
1-O_{p}(p^{-D})
for some large
D\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\left|-\lambda\bm{z}^{\top}(n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2})^{-1}\bm{\beta}+\frac{1}{n}\bm{z}^{\top}(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\beta}\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}\left(\frac{p^{\vartheta+1/2}}{n}\right).

Now we show that
\bm{z}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\epsilon\prec O_{p}(1)
by applying Markov’s inequality. Assume
\delta>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\bm{z}^{\top}\left(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}\right)^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\epsilon\right|\geq\varepsilon p^{-\delta}\right)\leq p^{2\delta}\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{z}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{z}\right)/\varepsilon^{2}.

Notice that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\bm{z}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{z}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\{\bm{z}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{z}\right\}-n\lambda\mathbb{E}\left\{\bm{z}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{I}_{p})^{-2}\bm{z}\right\}.

Similar to our previous argument, for some large enough
D
, with probability of
1-O_{p}(p^{-D})
, we have

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\left|\bm{z}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{z}-\frac{1}{n\lambda}\bm{z}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+m(-\lambda)\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-1}\bm{z}\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(\frac{p^{\vartheta}}{\lambda n})\quad\mbox{and}

(S.3)

\displaystyle\left|\bm{z}^{\top}\left(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+\lambda n\mathbb{I}_{p}\right)^{-2}\bm{z}-\frac{1}{n^{2}\lambda^{2}}\left\{\bm{z}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-1}\bm{z}-\lambda\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}_{n}\bm{z}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{z}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(\frac{p^{\vartheta}}{n^{2}}).

Therefore, with high probability, we have

\bm{z}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{z}=O_{p}(p^{-1})

and

\bm{z}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{I}_{p})^{-2}\bm{z}=O_{p}(p^{-2}).

Since
\bm{z}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{z}
and
\bm{z}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-2}\bm{z}
are both bounded, by bounded convergence theorem we have

p^{2\delta}\mathbb{E}\left\{\bm{z}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{z}\right\}/\varepsilon^{2}=O_{p}(p^{2\delta-1}).

By choosing
0<\delta<1/2
, with high probability, we have

\bm{z}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{I}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\epsilon\prec O_{p}(1).

It follows that

\frac{\left(\langle\bm{z},\widehat{\bm{\beta}}\rangle-\langle\bm{z},\bm{\beta}\rangle\right)^{2}}{\left\{\bm{z}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-1}\bm{\beta}\right\}^{2}}\overset{p}{\to}1.

Combining concentrations for
\left(\langle\bm{z},\widehat{\bm{\beta}}\rangle-\langle\bm{z},\bm{\beta}\rangle\right)^{2}
and
{\rm Tr}\left((\widehat{\bm{H}}-\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{2}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm Tr}\left((\widehat{\bm{H}}-\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{2}\right)\left(\langle\bm{z},\widehat{\bm{\beta}}\rangle-\langle\bm{z},\bm{\beta}\rangle\right)^{2}}{\left(\bm{z}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-1}\bm{\beta}\right)^{2}n^{-2}\lambda^{-2}\left\{{\rm Tr}\left(\left(\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-1}\right)-\lambda\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}{\rm Tr}\left(\left(\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\right)\right\}}\overset{p}{\to}1.

Task 3:

We provide the first-order concentration of
\|\bm{y}-\bm{X}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}\|_{2}^{2}
.
First, we decompose
\|\bm{y}-\bm{X}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}\|_{2}^{2}
as follows

\displaystyle\|\bm{y}-\bm{X}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{y}-2\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}

\displaystyle+\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}.

The concentration of the first term can be obtained by applying Proposition S
3
, where we have

\frac{\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{y}}{n\left(\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}+\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\right)}\overset{p}{\to}1.

The second term can be further decomposed into three simpler parts

\displaystyle\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}

\displaystyle=\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}

\displaystyle+2\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}.

Considering fixed
\bm{X}_{0}
, for
\forall\delta\in(0,1/2)
, the concentration for
\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}
can be obtained by applying Proposition S
4

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}\left|\sigma_{\epsilon}^{-2}\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}-{\rm Tr}\left(\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\right)\right|^{2}\leq C\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}|\epsilon|^{4}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}}{\rm Tr}\left(\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\implies\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}-\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}{\rm Tr}\left(\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\right)\right|<O_{p}(p^{1/2+\delta})\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{C\left(\mathbb{E}|\epsilon_{1}|^{4}{\rm Tr}\left(\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\right)\right)}{p^{1+2\delta}}=1-O_{p}(p^{-2\delta}).

Note that
{\rm Tr}(\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top})
is exactly
\widehat{df}
, which we have established its concentration in
eq.

S.2
. So every term with an order smaller than
\Theta_{p}(p)
would be negligible. This leads to the negligibility of
\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}
, which can be seen from the following Markov’s inequality

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}\right|<O_{p}(p^{1/2+\delta})\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-O_{p}\left(\frac{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}}{p^{1+2\delta}}\right)=1-O_{p}(p^{-2\delta}).

Therefore, with high probability over the randomness of
\bm{\epsilon}
, we can drop this interaction term.

We now turn to the concentration of
\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}
. This term can be further decomposed into the summation of three terms

\displaystyle\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}=\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}-n\lambda\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\beta}+n^{2}\lambda^{2}\bm{\beta}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{\beta}.

By Lemma S
8
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}}{n}-\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}\right|\leq O_{p}(p^{-\delta})\right)\geq 1-O_{p}\left(\frac{\|\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{4}}{np^{-2\delta}}\right)=1-O_{p}\left(p^{-1+2\delta}\right),

\displaystyle\implies\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}-n\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}\right|\leq O_{p}(p^{1-\delta})\right)\geq 1-O_{p}\left(p^{-1+2\delta}\right),

leading to the concentration for
\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}
.

Notice that
n\lambda\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\beta}
is deterministic and the concentration of
n^{2}\lambda^{2}\bm{\beta}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{\beta}
follows from the same argument as in
eq.

S.3
. Explicitly, for some large
D\in\mathbb{R}
, with probability of at least
1-O_{p}(p^{-D})
over the randomness of
\bm{X}_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\bm{\beta}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{\beta}-\frac{1}{\lambda n}\bm{\beta}(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\beta}\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(\frac{p^{\vartheta}}{\lambda n}).

It follows that

\displaystyle\frac{\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}}{n\left(\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}-\lambda\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\beta}+\lambda\bm{\beta}^{\top}(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\beta}\right)}\overset{p}{\to}1.

Combining all concentrations above, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\left\{p-{\rm Tr}\left((\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\right)\right\}+n\left\{\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}-\lambda\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\beta}+\lambda\bm{\beta}^{\top}(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\beta}\right\}}\overset{p}{\to}1.

Lastly, we turn to the concentration for
\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}
.

We consider the following decomposition

\displaystyle\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}=\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}-n\lambda\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}^{2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}.

We have alright obtained the concentration for
\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}
, so we only need to find the limit of
\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}^{2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}
, which is

\displaystyle\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}^{2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}=\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}^{2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}+2\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}^{2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}^{2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}.

Considering the randomness of
\bm{\epsilon}
, it is easy to check that
2\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}^{2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}
is negligible compared with
\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}^{2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}
and
\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}^{2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}
by Markov’s inequality. Furthermore, by Lemma S
4
, we have

\frac{\epsilon^{\top}\bm{X}R^{2}\bm{X}^{\top}\epsilon}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}{\rm Tr}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}R^{2})}\overset{p}{\to}1.

Notice that

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}^{2})={\rm Tr}(\bm{R})-n\lambda{\rm Tr}(\bm{R}^{2}).

By replacing
\bm{z}
with
\epsilon/\|\epsilon\|_{2}
in
eq.

S.3
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm Tr}(\bm{R})-n\lambda{\rm Tr}(\bm{R}^{2})}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}{\rm Tr}\left((\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\right)/n}\overset{p}{\to}1.

It follows that

\displaystyle\frac{\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}{\rm Tr}\left((\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\right)/n}\overset{p}{\to}1.

Similarly, we simplify
\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}R^{2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}
by further decomposition

\displaystyle\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}^{2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}=\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\beta}-2n\lambda\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{R}\bm{\beta}+n^{2}\lambda^{2}\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{R}^{2}\bm{\beta}.

We have already obtained the concentration for each of the three terms. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}^{2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}}{\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}^{\top}(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\beta}-\lambda\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}_{n}\bm{\beta}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}}\overset{p}{\to}1

It follows that

\displaystyle\frac{\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}^{2}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}}{\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}^{\top}(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\beta}-\lambda\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}_{n}\bm{\beta}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}+\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}{\rm Tr}\left((\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\right)/n}\overset{p}{\to}1.

Eventually, by combining all results above, we obtain the desired concentration for
\|\bm{y}-\bm{X}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\frac{\|\bm{y}-\bm{X}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}\|_{2}^{2}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\left\{n-p+{\rm Tr}\left((\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\right)-\lambda\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}{\rm Tr}\left((\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\right)\right\}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}n\lambda^{2}\bm{\beta}^{\top}(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}}\overset{p}{\to}1.

Task 4:
We consider the first-order concentration of the “de-biased” part.
We first explicitly write down the biasness of the ridge estimator as follows

\displaystyle\frac{\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{y}-\bm{X}\widehat{\bm{\beta}})}{n-\widehat{df}}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{n-\widehat{df}}}\left\{n\lambda\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n\lambda}{n-\widehat{df}}\left(\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}\bm{\beta}-n\lambda\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}\bm{R}\bm{\beta}+\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}\right).

It iseasy to check that
\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}
is negligible, and with the same argument as Lemma S
17
, we obtain the following concentration

\displaystyle\frac{\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}(\bm{y}-\bm{X}\widehat{\bm{\beta}})}{n-\widehat{df}}\overset{p}{\to}\frac{n\lambda}{n-p+{\rm Tr}\left((\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\right)}\left\langle\bm{z}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1},\bm{\beta}-(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\beta}\right\rangle.

Now, we are ready to provide the quantitative CLT for
\bm{z}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{R}}(\lambda)
. Recall that we denote

\displaystyle\mathfrak{h}=\phi_{n}\left\{{\rm Tr}\left(\left(\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-1}\right)-\lambda\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}{\rm Tr}\left(\left(\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\right)\right\}/p,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}=1-\phi_{n}\left\{1-\frac{{\rm Tr}\left((\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\right)}{p}\right\},

and
\sigma_{\text{R}}^{2}=(\sigma_{\text{R1}}^{2}+\sigma_{\text{R2}}^{2})/\mathfrak{g}^{2}
, where

\displaystyle\sigma_{\text{R1}}^{2}=\mathfrak{h}\left(z^{\top}(\mathbb{I}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\beta}\right)^{2}\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\sigma_{\text{R2}}^{2}=\left\{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\mathfrak{g}+\lambda\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}\left\{\lambda\bm{\beta}^{\top}(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}-\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}/n{\rm Tr}\left((\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\right)\right\}\right\}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\bm{z}\|_{2}^{2}.

Then under Assumptions
1
-S
1
, the following quantitative CLT holds

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma_{\text{R}}^{-1}\sqrt{n}\left\{\left\langle\bm{z},\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{R}}(\lambda)\right\rangle-\left\{\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{\beta}-\frac{\lambda}{\mathfrak{g}}\left\langle\bm{z},\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}\left(\bm{\beta}-(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\beta}\right)\right\rangle\right\}\right\}<t\right)-\Phi(t)\right|\to 0.

Appendix S.10
Proof for Section
S.2.2

To prove the quantitative CLT of
A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{R}}(\lambda))
in Theorem S
2
, we use eight steps, employing a leave-one-out technique.
Briefly, we decompose the numerator of
A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{R}}(\lambda))
and provide the quantitative CLT regarding the randomness of
\bm{\epsilon}_{z}
and
\bm{Z}_{0}
. Then, by adopting the CLT for the de-biased estimator, we consider the randomness of
\bm{X}_{0}
and
\bm{\epsilon}
to obtain the corresponding quantitative CLTs. Using the quantitative CLT of quadratic forms, we account for the randomness of
\bm{\beta}
. Finally, we obtain the first-order limit of the denominator, and the quantitative CLT of
A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{R}}(\lambda))
follows from Slutsky’s theorem.

S.10.1
Numerator decomposition

The numerator of
A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\text{R}}(\lambda))
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\left(\bm{Z}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon}_{z}\right)^{\top}\bm{Z}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}

\displaystyle=\bm{\beta}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}+\bm{\epsilon}_{z}^{\top}\bm{Z}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}.

This decomposition enables us to conduct sequentially conditioning.
Adopting notations in Theorem S
2
, in this section we denote

\sigma^{2}=n_{z}\left(\tau_{1}+2\tau_{2}^{2}+\tau_{3}+\tau_{4}^{2}\right)

for simplicity.

S.10.2
Berry-Esseen bounds with the randomness of testing error

Conditional on
\bm{Z}_{0}
,
\bm{X}_{0}
,
\bm{\epsilon}
, and
\bm{\beta}
, and consider the randomness of
\bm{\epsilon}_{z}
. By Lemma S
6
, we have the following Berry-Esseen bound

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\langle\bm{\epsilon}_{z},\bm{Z}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\rangle}{\sigma_{\epsilon_{z}}\sqrt{\|\bm{Z}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\|_{2}^{2}}}<t\right)-\Phi_{\epsilon_{z}}(t)\right|

(S.1)

\displaystyle\leq O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n_{z}}\left\{\bm{Z}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\right\}^{4}_{i}}{\|\bm{Z}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\|_{2}^{4}}}\right).

For simplicity, we denote

\sigma_{1}^{2}=\sigma_{\epsilon_{z}}^{2}\|\bm{Z}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\|_{2}^{2}.

Considering the following CDF

\displaystyle\bm{H}_{\text{R}}(t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}+\bm{\epsilon}_{z}^{\top}\bm{Z}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}-\tau_{0}}{\sigma}<t\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{\epsilon}_{z}^{\top}\bm{Z}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}}{\sigma_{1}}\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\sigma}<t-\frac{\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}-\tau_{0}}{\sigma}\right).

Recall that we denote
\Lambda_{\epsilon_{z}}
as the standard Gaussian random variable replies only on the randomness of
\bm{\epsilon}_{z}
. Then by applying
eq.

S.1
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\bm{H}_{\text{R}}(t)-\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\sigma}\Lambda_{\epsilon_{z}}<t-\frac{\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}-\tau_{0}}{\sigma}\right)\right|

(S.2)

\displaystyle\leq O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n_{z}}\left\{\bm{Z}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\right\}^{4}_{i}}{\|\bm{Z}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\|_{2}^{4}}}\right).

S.10.3
Berry-Esseen bounds with the randomness of testing data matrix

Conditional on
\bm{X}_{0}
,
\bm{\epsilon}
,
\bm{\beta}
, we consider the randomness of
\bm{Z}_{0}
in this section.
We first define the subset where quantities related to
\bm{Z}_{0}
concentrate properly, and further show such concentration holds with high probability. For
\forall\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}>0
and
0<\delta<1/2
, we denote the subset:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2})\coloneqq

\displaystyle\quad\left\{\bm{Z}_{0}:\left\{\left|\|\bm{Z}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\|_{2}^{2}-n_{z}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\|_{2}^{2}\right|<p^{1/2+\delta}\varepsilon_{1}\right\}\cap\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\left\{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{z}}\left\{\bm{Z}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\right\}^{4}_{i}-n_{z}\mathbb{E}\left|z_{0_{i}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\right|^{4}\right|<p^{1/2+\delta}\varepsilon_{2}\right\}\right\}.

By Lemma S
7
, we have the following inequalities

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\|\bm{Z}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\|_{2}^{2}-n_{z}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\|_{2}^{2}\right|<p^{1/2+\delta}\varepsilon_{1}\right)\geq 1-O_{p}\left(\frac{p\mathbb{E}\left|\bm{Z}_{0_{i}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\right|^{4}}{p^{1+2\delta}\varepsilon_{1}^{2}}\right)\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n_{z}}\left\{\bm{Z}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\right\}^{4}_{i}-n_{z}\mathbb{E}\left|\bm{Z}_{0_{i}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\right|^{4}\right|<p^{1/2+\delta}\varepsilon_{2}\right)\geq 1-O_{p}\left(\frac{p\mathbb{E}\left|\bm{Z}_{0_{i}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\right|^{8}}{p^{1+2\delta}\varepsilon_{2}^{2}}\right).

Moreover, notice that
\mathbb{E}\left|\bm{z}_{0_{i}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\right|^{4}=O_{p}(1)
and
\mathbb{E}\left|\bm{z}_{0_{i}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\right|^{8}=O_{p}(1)
. Therefore, we have
\mathbb{P}(\bm{Z}_{0}\in\Gamma_{1})\geq 1-O_{p}(p^{-2\delta})
. For
\bm{Z}_{0}\in\Gamma_{1}
, we can simplify the Berry-Esseen upper bound in

eq.

S.2
as follows

\displaystyle O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n_{z}}(\bm{z}_{0_{i}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y})^{4}}{\|\bm{Z}\bm{R}\ \bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\|_{2}^{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n_{z}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(z_{0}^{4}-3\right)\sum_{i=1}^{p}(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y})_{i}^{4}+3(\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y})^{2}\right\}+O_{p}(p^{1/2+\delta})}{n_{z}^{2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\|_{2}^{4}+O_{p}(p^{3/2+\delta})}}\right).

Considering the randomness of
\bm{Z}_{0}
and by Lemma S
2
, we have the following inequality

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}-n_{z}\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}}{\sigma_{2}}<t\right)-\Phi_{\bm{Z}_{0}}(t)\right|

(S.3)

\displaystyle\leq O_{p}(p^{-1/2}),

where

\sigma_{2}^{2}=n_{z}\left\{\mathbb{E}(z_{0}^{4}-3)\sum_{i=1}^{p}(\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta})_{i}^{2}\left(\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\right)_{i}^{2}+2\left(\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\right)^{2}+\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}.

Rearranging the terms in second probability of
eq.

S.2
and applying
eq.

S.3
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}-n_{z}\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}}{\sigma}<t-\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\sigma}\Lambda_{\epsilon_{z}}-\frac{n_{z}\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}-\tau_{0}}{\sigma}\right)-\right.

(S.4)

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\sigma_{2}}{\sigma}\Lambda_{\bm{Z}_{0}}<t-\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{\epsilon_{z}}^{2}n_{z}\|\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}\|_{2}^{2}+O_{p}(p^{1/2+\delta})}}{\sigma}\Lambda_{\epsilon_{z}}-\frac{n_{z}\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}-\tau_{0}}{\sigma}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq O_{p}\left(\max(p^{-1/2},p^{-2\delta})\right).

S.10.4
First-order concentrations with the randomness of training error and training data matrix

Conditional on
\bm{\beta}
, we provide first-order concentrations for the following two quantities that are involved in the fluctuation of Gaussian random variables

1.

\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}
and

2.

\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}.

S.10.4.1
Concentration for the first quantity

In this section, we provide concentration for
\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}
in three steps.

Step 1:
Decomposing
\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}
, we have

\displaystyle\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}

(S.5)

\displaystyle=\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}+2\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}.

Step 2:

We first show that the interaction term in
eq.

S.5
,
\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}
, is negligible with high probability, as it has order lower than
\Theta_{p}(1)
. By Markov’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}\right|>\varepsilon_{3}p^{-\delta}\right)\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}\right)}{\varepsilon_{3}^{2}p^{-2\delta}}.

Note that the numerator of the right-hand side is of order
O_{p}(p^{-1})
. Therefore, for
\forall\delta\in(0,1/2)
, we have

\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}=O_{p}(p^{-\delta})

with probability of at least
1-O_{p}(p^{2\delta-1})
.
Our question now boils down to finding the concentrations of
\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}
and
\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}
, the first two terms in
eq.

S.5
.

Step 3:
We provide the first-order concentration for
\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}
.
Note that

\displaystyle\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}

\displaystyle=\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}-2n\lambda\bm{\beta}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}+\lambda^{2}n^{2}\bm{\beta}^{\top}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{\beta}.

Similar to our arguments in Lemma S
17
and
eq.

S.10
, with probability of at least
1-O_{p}(p^{-D})
for some large
D\in\mathbb{R}
and any small
\vartheta>0
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}-\frac{1}{n\lambda}\bm{\beta}^{\top}(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}\right|\leq O_{p}(p^{-3/2+\vartheta})\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\left|\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\beta}-\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}}{n^{2}\lambda^{2}\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{2}}\bm{\beta}^{\top}(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}\right|\leq O_{p}(p^{-5/2+\vartheta}).

Therefore, with probability of at least
1-O_{p}(p^{-D})
, we have

\displaystyle\Bigg|\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}-\Big\{\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}-2\bm{\beta}\left(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}+\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{2}}\bm{\beta}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}\Big\}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq O_{p}(p^{-1/2+\vartheta}).

Step 4:
We provide the first-order concentration for
\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}
.
We show that, by fixing
\bm{X}_{0}
,
\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}
concentrates regarding the randomness of
\bm{\epsilon}
.
We first compute

\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}\right)=\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}{\rm Tr}\left(\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\right).

Denote the fixed matrix

\bm{M}=\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top},

for
\forall\delta\in(0,1/2),\forall\varepsilon_{4}>0
, by Markov’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{M}\bm{\epsilon}-\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}{\rm Tr}(\bm{M})\right|>p^{-\delta}\varepsilon_{4}\right)\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{M}\bm{\epsilon}\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{M}\bm{\epsilon}\right]-\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}{\rm Tr}(\bm{M})^{2}}{\varepsilon_{4}^{2}p^{-2\delta}}.

After careful computation, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{M}\bm{\epsilon}\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{M}\bm{\epsilon}\right]-\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}{\rm Tr}(\bm{M})^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left(\epsilon^{4}-3\right)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{M}_{i,i}^{2}+2\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}{\rm Tr}(\bm{M}^{2}).

Here
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{M}_{i,i}^{2}\preceq{\rm Tr}(\bm{M}^{2})
, and note that
{\rm Tr}(\bm{M}^{2})=O_{p}(p^{-1})
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\epsilon^{\top}\bm{M}\epsilon-\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}{\rm Tr}(\bm{M})\right|>p^{-\delta}\varepsilon_{4}\right)\leq O_{p}(p^{2\delta-1}).

Furthermore, we can decompose
{\rm Tr}(\bm{M})
as follows

{\rm Tr}\left(\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\right)={\rm Tr}\left(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\right)-n\lambda{\rm Tr}\left(\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\right).

By the anisotropic local law
[
35
]
,
similar to our argument in
eq.

S.1
,
with probability of at least
1-O_{p}(p^{-D+1})
for some large enough
D>1
and some small enough
\vartheta>0
,
we have

\displaystyle\left|{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R})-\frac{1}{n\lambda}{\rm Tr}\left((\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}\right)\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(p^{\vartheta}).

Similar to our argument in
eq.

S.10
, with probability of at least
1-O_{p}(p^{-D+1})
, we have

\displaystyle\left|{\rm Tr}\left(\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\right)-\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}}{n^{2}\lambda^{2}\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{2}}{\rm Tr}\left((\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\right)\right|\leq\psi(-\lambda)O_{p}(p^{\vartheta-1}).

Combining these results above, for
\forall\delta\in(0,1/2)
, with probability of at least
1-O_{p}(p^{2\delta-1})
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\bm{\epsilon}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}-\frac{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}{n\lambda}\left\{{\rm Tr}\left((\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}\right)-\frac{\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}_{n}}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{2}}{\rm Tr}\left((\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\right)\right\}\right|\leq O_{p}(p^{-\delta}).

Therefore, for
\forall\delta\in(0,1/2)
, with probability of at least
1-O_{p}(p^{2\delta-1})
, the following concentration inequality holds

\displaystyle\left|\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}-\left\{\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}-2\bm{\beta}\left(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}+\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{2}}\bm{\beta}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\qquad+\frac{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}{n\lambda}\left\{{\rm Tr}\left((\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}\right)-\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{2}}{\rm Tr}\left((\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\right)\right\}\right\}\right|\leq O_{p}(p^{-\delta}).

S.10.4.2
Concentration for the second quantity

In this section, we provide concentration for
\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}
in three steps.

Step 1:
Decomposing
\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}=\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}
, we have

\displaystyle\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}

\displaystyle=\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}=\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}-n\lambda\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\beta}+\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}.

Step 2:
We first show that the interaction term
\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}
is negligible
with high probability. By Markov’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}\right|>\varepsilon_{5}p^{-\delta}\right)\leq\frac{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}R\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}R\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}\right]}{\varepsilon_{5}^{2}p^{-2\delta}}

for
\forall\delta\in(0,1/2)
and
\forall\varepsilon_{5}>0
.
Note that
\mathbb{E}\left(\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}\right)=O_{p}(1)
, with probability of at least
1-O_{p}(p^{-1+2\delta})
, we have

\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}(\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+n\lambda\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p})^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}=O_{p}(p^{-\delta}).

Therefore,
\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{\epsilon}
is negligible with high probability.

Step 3:

Similar to our argument in
eq.

S.1
, for
\forall\vartheta>0
small enough, with probability of at least
1-O_{p}(p^{-D})
, we have

\displaystyle\left|n\lambda\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}^{\top}(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}\right|\leq O_{p}(p^{-1/2+\vartheta})

\displaystyle\implies\left|\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}-\left\{\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}-\bm{\beta}^{\top}(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma})^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}\right\}\right|\leq O_{p}(p^{-1/2+\vartheta}).

Now define the subset where quantities related to
\bm{\epsilon}
and
\bm{X}_{0}
concentrates properly, which is

\displaystyle\Gamma_{2}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\left\{\bm{X}_{0},\epsilon:\left\{\left|\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{R}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{R}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{y}-\left\{\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}-2\bm{\beta}\left(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}+\frac{\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{2}}\bm{\beta}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{\bm{I}}_{p}+\mathfrak{m}_{n}\bm{\Sigma}\right)^{-2}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\beta}\right.\right.\right.\right.
